## Handover chain, in order. Do not duplicate these blocks.

3. **The Volume 03 thread list at the END of this file** — the live threads after Chapter 110, in the volume's own register.

Nobody was lied to. The design is the harm, and the design is a calendar.** |

## Volume 01 Closed — Guardrails still binding in Volume 02

- **Volume 01 ended at Chapter 48 with the notice `Permission: find the blank bearer.` on the Old Ward board. Volume 02 has not restated, answered or explained it, and must not open a chapter by reissuing it as a system message.**
- The volume's calendar is anchored at Chapter 21 = Thursday 13 March and was never recomputed from the real calendar. **19 May is a Monday. 20 May is a Tuesday. 26 May is a Monday. 29 May is a Thursday. 18 July is a Friday. Do not recompute.**
- Standing dates ahead of Chapter 70: the **west-clinic 22:00 handovers continue daily and are short**; the **19 May sitting is over and Schedule 4 is referred on to the next sitting, whose date the Committee has not fixed**; **Royden Achebe's 6-L/14 report is due Thursday 29 May**; the **claims box closes Friday 18 July**; Jonas is on nothing.
- Keep the west-clinic gate running on its local term. It **cannot** be renewed by Jonas, the department or a manager.
- The 11:00–13:30 gap, Bram Osei's unfilled Thursday and the two-refusal rule all stay open and dated. **The retainer is paid; the damage was a witness, and the first hollow term was written by another one.**
- **The Weir stand has no term, no holder and no claim, and the trap can no longer be cleared by anybody.** Nobody may add a name to that rota. Bo Trimble is findable and nothing may be added to him. The bank line has a ring round a blank space and may not be filled by a speech. **Margo Doré has said out loud that she wants the street left with nothing on it, and a pump has not been offered yet.**
- Lorna Vessey's released slot, Marta Sowle's fall, Ruth Nwosu's unrecorded hours, the nine minutes of 24 March, the nineteen of 3 April and the forty minutes of 15 April are all on the public box and none is discharged. **The forty minutes is time until the family knew, not the time the door took. There is no 48-minute figure in this account and no later volume may print one.**
- **Do not open Selah Mora's sealed log, copy resident identities, or put the Weir's tin or the market's brass tag into a file.**
- **Do not explain the Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the watch term's origin, Evan, or Iona. Do not read a root clause. Volume 02 contains no root reading, no root clause and no succession.**
- Do not resolve the Weir's three-way disagreement, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, or the name in biro on the map, and do not let Jonas choose for the Weir.
- **Keep System language sparing: at most one short notice per ordinary chapter, on a civic maintenance surface, never choosing the solution, never naming a person.** The ceiling was exceeded once in Ch 46 and logged. **Volume 02 Chapters 49–59 used five notices across eleven chapters and exceeded it in none.**
- **No death, no rescue by chance.** Six bottles were carried up a hundred and nine steps by a woman of sixty-one who is not on any rota at ten to five in the morning, and nobody wrote it down, and if anybody had she would have asked them to take it out.

## Volume 02 — Open questions the next batch must carry

**Chapters 71–80. The heat, the narrowings, the first transfer that has to be repeated, and a cooperative steward board that does not exist yet.**

1. **The heat.** A **Borough Heat Threshold Protocol** is pinned to the Old Ward board: activation on three consecutive days at 27°C or above, requiring residential care premises to **confirm occupancy and a named responsible person for every address in their custody.** It was twenty-nine on 19 May and the next four days are forecast at thirty, thirty-one, twenty-nine and twenty-eight. **That is Schedule 4's logic arriving as health and safety, and it asks a landlord to produce in a form the thing Marguerite Oyelaran spent a month refusing to let a form produce.** Four hundred and twenty women in this borough have their instruments out of one decontamination room on level zero, whose supplier's service contract now runs on £410 and twenty minutes a week that nobody has taken. Spring tides: 21, 22, 28, 29 May and 1 June.
2. **06:00 Sunday 12 October 2014**, when the Saint Orra west schedule expires, and **the 22:00 handover on Saturday the eleventh of October is the crossing**: a six-day term read then begins at 07:00 on the twelfth, one hour after the money stops, and two tired porters will read it and will not know. **Tomas Renn is going to ask the two-refusal question with that date on it, in the shop on Monday 19 May and then in a corridor at Saint Orra west on Thursday 22 May, and Jonas has been told twice not to come.**
3. **The first transfer that has to be repeated.** Jonas's name is on the 17 May record as the man who moved it. Achebe cannot hand the claim back. In August somebody will ring him about a Tuesday, and **legal responsibility follows the transfer and not the person who received it**, and he learns that the difference is the whole of transfer stewardship and it is not a gift.
4. **Royden Achebe's 6-L/14 report is due 29 May** and the bank is gone and the trap is lost and the burden is his, and it will land in the same week as the heat.
5. **The September record the chair has promised**, in which a number of nodes in this city have **no party answering for them**, is owed and not yet written, and there is a form with a hole in it and a word *nobody* in Nina Calder's hand.
6. **Margo Doré, who has not been told that a Tier 1 price on six lowland water points is in a public record in the Old Ward**, and who has said she wants the street left with nothing on it. This is live and unpaid.
7. **The Borough Heat Threshold Protocol and the return both ask the same question — who is the named responsible person for an address — and **the one body in this city that can answer it for a hundred and twenty-eight rooms has spent a month refusing to let anybody ask it of her.**
8. **The cooperative steward board**, which does not exist yet, was named and refused in one sentence in the 19 May transcript and **may not be formed in this batch.**

## Standing guardrails for Chapters 71–80

- **One Lattice notice per ordinary chapter at most.** Four were used in Chapters 60–70, in Chapters 61, 65, 68 and 70. The Ch 46 ceiling was not repeated and is not to be.
- **No death, no rescue by chance, no lucky timing.** Nobody is saved by luck in this batch either.
- **Do not tidy the Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, or the blank line on the form.** **Do not resolve the Weir's three-way disagreement and do not let Jonas choose it. A pump may be offered; Margo Doré may refuse it in her own words; it may not be argued out of her.**
- **Keep the west-clinic gate running on its local term. It cannot be renewed by Jonas, the department, or a manager.** The rota has still not been built again, the terms are still six days, the 11:00–13:30 gap is still published and unfilled, and **Bram Osei's refused Thursday is still on the board and he asked that it stay, and Priya Ranganathan has said she is not going to be the person who asks him for a second refusal on a Sunday so that there are two of them on a board.**
- **The forty minutes of 15 April is time until the family knew, not the time the door took, and no 48-minute figure may ever be printed.**
- **The claims box closes Friday 18 July and nothing may be written into it that establishes a service or a person.**
- **A node with no term in force is not a person with no address. Do not slide from one into the other.** A duty is an act and not a place, and that is the instrument, and it is not a recognition of a street or of its people.
- **The cooperative steward board is the end of the volume, not a device of the next batch. It may be named, argued about, or refused. It may not be formed here.**
- **The register of attachment is a bucket under a tap and it is now on a record in a file as the name of the man who moved a duty. He may not be appointed, made acting, shadowed, or released by no person.**

# After Chapter 80 — Volume 02, Batch 0003 (the heat)

## Live and dated, and it must be carried into Batch 0004

1. **THURSDAY 19 JUNE 2014, TEN IN THE MORNING, COMMITTEE ROOM TWO: the next sitting of the Standing Committee on Civic Continuity now has a date.** The returns are to be read into the record as they are received. The definition of *holder* is on the papers if and when a definition is before the Committee, and the chair has said she will not arrange one. **Fourteen returns have been answered and are in two drawers, and eight of them are on Nina Calder's form and four on the borough's and two on both, and the two on both answer for the same meter room.** This is the first clock in this volume and the whole city now has one.
2. **06:00 SUNDAY 12 OCTOBER 2014** — the Saint Orra west schedule expires. **THE 22:00 HANDOVER ON SATURDAY 11 OCTOBER IS THE CROSSING** and it is a Saturday night, and a term read then begins at 07:00 on the twelfth, one hour after the money stops. **Nothing has happened about it since 22 May.** Tomas Renn has the date, the question has been asked in a shop and in a corridor, and no rule exists. The two-refusal date is **the eleventh of October, not the twelfth.**
3. **In August somebody will ring Jonas Mercer about a Tuesday, and the man who will ring him is Royden Achebe**, who has known that since 18 May, offered the claim back inside the first hour of a seven-day window and used no minute of the rest, and has written that the only thing left in this account that is his to do about it is to be the one who rings. **Jonas still does not know whether he is going to be the one who answers. That decision has not been made and must not be made early.**
4. **THE DEEPEST REFUSAL IN THE BATCH IS UNPAID AND DATED: the decontamination verification log on level zero at Saint Orra west has carried no name and no signature since 9 May 2014 and the weekly twenty minutes has not happened once.** The supplier's quarterly visit is in July. **If nothing is signed before then there is a file with no signature in it and a compliance paragraph in the autumn in front of four hundred and twenty women. Dr Sanaa Iqbal refused to be the standing name and said so on the record, and it is the finding of the 9 June review, and it has not been argued out of her.** Gunnar Halvorsen has not asked anybody. Jonas Mercer asked the one question he was allowed at the review and got the true answer.
5. **The 11:00–13:30 gap is still published and unfilled. Bram Osei's refused Thursday is still on the board in his own words. Priya Ranganathan's name is off the funded line as of 1 June and she has asked for it to stay on the record of who held it, and it goes in the minutes unedited.** The terms are still six days. The gate still runs on its local term and cannot be renewed by Jonas, the department, or a manager.
6. **The 6-L/14 line is on the field services file with `reported not maintainable, release recommended, not released` against it, awaiting a level-three acceptance that will be a signature, in about eleven weeks.** Four tonnes of stone and a sill beam, about £1,180, and no civic claim attaches, so there is nobody to invoice. **Nina Calder's sentence about 2011 and a bucket under a tap is unasked-for, unreturned, and unspent.**
7. **The 19 June sitting is the chair's September record arriving early and in the wrong shape**, and the September quarterly report of the Saint Orra and District Flood Forum is still owed, with Nora Bell's name in the attendance column and her words in it unedited, and the eleventh place still empty and still not written *vacant*.
8. **Marguerite Oyelaran has been paid a price in public and has said the fee does not stop.** The association's answers are now in a public document: **106 occupied addresses, 122 on the rent roll, 92 rooms actually paid for, a difference of sixteen that is a void and reads as bad management, and a difference of fourteen that is eight households carrying water in buckets and six who never signed, which is on nothing because every document that could hold it is a list of who does not pay.** She will not produce a list and the answer to any request for a list of the people in her stock is two rooms above a betting shop on Carrow Lane. **The shortfall is £4,361.60 and the void allowance on Block C is nil in October and nobody has fixed the freeholder's four meters or written the one letter eight households have asked for twice in sixteen years.**
9. **The cooperative steward board has now been named twice, in a Committee transcript in May and in a records office on 2 June, and refused twice, and the second refusal was for a better reason than money, and NOTHING WAS FORMED.** Frances Tolley found section 11 of the 2008 regulations in an hour and a half and copied it out by hand. Pell's reason is page nine. **The chair has refused in writing terms to constitute anything on the strength of a permissive power found in a corridor.** The condition Nina Calder stated — that one of the nine has to be able to be put in a room and told to stop, and removed in front of the other eight, by people who did not put him there — has not been answered and she has asked anybody who disagrees to say so in writing before 19 June.
10. **The Weir, untouched and still to be touched as a decision.** Margo Doré has refused a pump **in advance, in her own words, on 1 June, to a man who came for nothing and not to a pump**, and nobody argued. She knows about the £11,280 in the minute and has said she is not angry with him, which is not the same as forgiven and is not a resolution. **The trap is lost for good, the bank is gone, the three positions stand, the ring of pencil has nothing in it, nobody is on the rota, and Bo Trimble is findable and nothing may be added to him.**

## Untouched, unspent, and forbidden in Section D

- **The nineteen of thirty-one at Wren Court, Salter's Yard**, and Yolanda Fofana's own signature lapsed on 1 April. **No chapter in 49–80 touched it and it may not be resolved before Volume 03.** The Wren Court fee does not stop.
- The burnt letter in the cook-house stove. The boy of nineteen on the bicycle. The name in biro on the map in the coat, folded with the margin inward. Prue Calloway's location-of-record finding. The Saint Orra west rota. Selah Mora's sealed log and the tin on the shelf and the brass tag in an apron pocket. Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause and Raynor-Hale's September report. The Boy of the annex. The retainer, still *not yet agreed*.
- **The Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the origin of the community-watch term, the three-line brass tokens, Evan Mercer's counter-amendment and second signature, Iona Vey, the root chamber, the blank bearer, any root reading, any root clause, any succession.** None of it was touched in Chapters 49–80 and none of it may be touched in Section D.
- **A priced-access case is not a recognition case. A node with no term in force is not a person with no address. A duty is an act and not a place.** Section D is the volume's method and the method is the chain, the node with a person in its holder field, that person instructing the mending, and Jonas refusing to do it himself.
- The claims box closes **Friday 18 July 2014** and holds one card about a stair. Nothing may be written into it that establishes a service or a person.
- The forty minutes of 15 April is time until the family knew, not the time the door took, and **no 48-minute figure may ever be printed.** The forty *hours* of 3 April is the gate being open and is a different number about a different thing.
- **One Lattice notice per ordinary chapter at most, and four across any ten chapters at most.** Four were used in 71–80, in Chapters 72, 74, 76 and 79. The condition about a heat protocol's named responsible person was **not** printed and no plate was manufactured carrying it.

# After Chapter 90 — Volume 02, Batch 0004 (Section D, Revocation)

## Live and dated, and it must be carried into Batch 0005

1. **WEDNESDAY 1 OCTOBER 2014.** Ninety days from service at 11:00 on **Thursday 3 July 2014**, the day the entry was made — ninety days from the third of July is the first of October and no other date is the expiry. **The Aldbrook Trust may re-assert the claim `T3/11400/0304`, or any part of it, at Tier 3, at that node, without the consent of any person whose name appears in the holder field of that node at any time before that date.** The trust has not noticed yet. A machine answered the letter on Friday 4 July and a person answered it on Monday 7 July, and the person is sixty-three and she signed the assignment of the second of May. **And Tomas Renn's question, asked in a shop on 8 July and unanswered: what happens on the day after, when she is in the field again because she has to be, and a consent can be given twice — which is not defeating the clause, it is complying with it, twice.**
2. **06:00 MONDAY 4 AUGUST 2014.** Two dates in one. (a) **The Borough Heat Threshold Protocol return is due**, seventy days after activation on 26 May, from a housing association whose first answer was one hundred and six in a ring-number box, cost a clerk of sixty-one an afternoon, and put a chief officer's name in a public document. **The chief officer has not been telephoned by Jonas since 31 May and that is the fifth time.** (b) **KH-4/20 lapses at 06:00** — eleven patients and a day room above a residential floor that was put on Tier 2 at £4,200 on 29 May by a trust that was covering a hole in its own budget, and nobody has re-priced it and nobody is required to.
3. **THURSDAY 17 JULY 2014, the non-conformance on the decontamination log.** Fifty-two Sundays, no signature on any. A paragraph number now exists on a supplier's system, the acceptance is about eleven weeks away, and the annual compliance report comes in the autumn in front of four hundred and twenty women. **Sanaa Iqbal's refusal stands, has not been argued out of her, and the engineer himself said that anybody in the building can sign it and that it is nobody's job, and nobody signed it.** The finding of 9 June explains why and the finding is a governance document and must not be produced as a compliance document.
4. **THE CLAIMS BOX IS CLOSED** (Friday 18 July). One card about a stair, sealed, dated, in a drawer on a lower floor. The box is empty. Nothing was written into it. **The envelope is a live object and nobody has opened it.**
5. **A LETTER IS OWED TO EIGHT HOUSEHOLDS** and has been asked for twice since about 2008, the second time about six years ago, and it went to a company and not to a person. **A woman telephoned the shop on 11 July at 16:25, gave no name, said she is one of the eight, and said there is no date on it and that is why she telephoned a shop. He has the number and has not rung it back. The letter is still the cheapest thing in this account and still unwritten.**
6. **NINA CALDER'S FOURTH PAGE** exists, one slip, one week, one number, and a person may refuse to be counted and the count then says how many refused. **She has a fifth one in her head and is not writing it down, and if she writes it down it becomes a scheme.** Nobody has asked her what the fourth page is for and she has not said.
7. **THE COOPERATIVE STEWARD BOARD.** Named twice, refused twice, **mentioned a third time on 9 July in a corridor by a registrar who has read section 11, and not constituted, staffed, minuted or promised.** Frances Tolley has a copy of section 11 in her own hand. **Nina Calder's condition is still unanswered: for nine seats to be anything other than nine chairs, one of the nine has to be able to be put in a room and told to stop, and removed in front of the other eight, by people who did not put him there.** And in a workshop in Salter's Yard on the night of 4 July, thirty-one people worked out in four sentences that they are not a body, cannot be seated, and cannot be a steward board either — which is Section E's problem stated by the people it is about, and it has not been answered.
8. **THE SEPTEMBER QUARTERLY REPORT** goes to print in three weeks with a section headed `lowland evacuation capacity: unresolved` and nothing under it, unless a sentence goes there. **Nine hundred and forty is a number of times and not a number of people and may never become a number of people.** The count of nodes with nobody answering for them is owed to the chair and has not been produced, and Jonas refused to manufacture it on 10 July in a room with a man who had a deadline. **The chair's September record of nodes with no party answering for them is still owed.** Priority access is not yet ruled invalid in any public case. **The council flood forum's eleventh place is empty and is not written *vacant*, and Nora Bell's name is in the tenth and in the attendance column.**
9. **06:00 SUNDAY 12 OCTOBER 2014, and the crossing at 22:00 on SATURDAY 11 OCTOBER.** **Nothing has happened about it since 22 May, and on 8 July Tomas Renn and Jonas Mercer both refused, separately and correctly, to discuss it in a shop, and the rule between them now is that neither of them will.** It is not resolved and it may not be staged again.
10. **A Tuesday in August.** Royden Achebe will ring this shop about a Tuesday and Jonas has known since 18 May that it is him and still has not decided whether he will be the one who answers, and that decision has not been made and must not be made early.
11. **6-L/14: the level-three tray.** Signed about 13 August. `reported not maintainable, release recommended, not released` stands, and a line like that does not sit on a file for a year.

## Fixed in this batch, and binding

- **The chain, all five links, and who signed what on what date.** See the canon block in `state/continuity.md`.
- **What a valid assignment transfers** — the claim, what is owed on it, and the duty of it — and that a share of a claim carries a tier and not the work, and is enforceable in respect of the tier only.
- **Pell's rule:** where the man who found the chain is also the mender, the standing must be given twice, aloud, in the room, after the chain is known, and the second time is the one that is entered.
- **Pell's ruling of 9 July:** a claim discharged for the holder is not discharged for the person who caused it, and findability is not answerability, and nothing in it exonerates anybody. **And there is no mechanism in this city to make the top of a chain pay and none to make a person take a duty, and those are the same sentence, and who pays for the refusal is nobody.**
- **Wren Court is resolved and it is the volume's payment.** See the canon block. **The fee does not stop. It is thirteen pounds sixty. The seventy-two pounds a month stopped and went back to Kestrel Hill. £2,431.16 stopped coming out of a workshop. A riser is two years away. Clause nine was read out four times and not tidied. Nothing was signed and there is no minute. The arrangement that survives is one findable person holding a thing that none of the thirty-one people whose water it is can ask her a single question about.**
- **The exact terms of the 19 June determination and the two entries for one meter room**, which cannot be corrected and will not be.
- **Nina Calder wrote on the fourth page.** She did not burn it. She put it in a bin on 4 July and took it out on 7 July.
- **The Tier 3 claim was REVOKED, not released and not dissolved**, on the ground of the last holder's consent, under section nine of the 2008 regulations, and the chain is broken in the middle.

## Untouched, unspent, and forbidden in Section E

- The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, the blank line at the bottom of the form, the claim at 6-L/14, **Margo Doré's advance refusal of a pump of 1 June**, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause, the Saint Orra west rota, Prue Calloway's location-of-record finding, Selah Mora's sealed log, the tin on the shelf, the brass tag in an apron pocket.
- **The Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the origin of the community-watch term, the three-line brass tokens, Evan Mercer's counter-amendment and second signature, Iona Vey, the root chamber, the blank bearer, any root reading, any root clause, any succession.**
- **A priced-access case is not a recognition case. A node with no term in force is not a person with no address. A duty is an act and not a place.** The woman at the bottom of Quarry Street has a fact about a node and not a name in anything, and Jonas deliberately never learned it.
- **No 48-minute figure may ever be printed.** The forty minutes of 15 April is time until the family knew.
- **Nine hundred and forty is a number of times.**
- **One Lattice notice per ordinary chapter at most, four across any ten chapters at most.** Two were printed in 81–90 (Ch 82, Ch 85) and two were restatements of a spent condition (Ch 89 spoken, Ch 90 printed in truncated and marked form). The condition about a named responsible person for an address has still never been printed and no plate has been manufactured carrying it.

# After Chapter 100 — VOLUME 02 IS CLOSED

## 1. The two dates. Both are after the last chapter and neither is in this volume.

**WEDNESDAY 1 OCTOBER 2014 — two days after the volume closes.** A ninety-day notice served at 11:00 on Thursday 3 July on five parties expires. The placing party may re-assert `T3/11400/0304`, or any part of it, at Tier 3, at Meter Room 3, Salter's Yard, **without the consent of any person whose name appears in the holder field of that node at any time before that date.** The Aldbrook Trust has not noticed that it has been notified; a machine answered on 4 July and Dorothy Cresswell, 63, answered on 7 July and will do her job with the notice in October. **The 28 August determination did not touch it and could not and said so in terms.** What happens on the first is not in Volume 02 and is not to be decided in advance by any later volume either — it is a date that the city will arrive at.

**TOMAS RENN'S QUESTION ABOUT THE DAY AFTER IS UNANSWERED AND IS THE SERIES' CURRENT LIVE QUESTION.** *What happens on the day after, when she is in the field again because she has to be, and a consent can be given twice — which is not defeating the clause, it is complying with it, twice.* **On 24 September 2014 Yolanda Fofana declined to answer it, gave the reason, and told Jonas Mercer not to write it down. He did not write it down. He wrote down that he was told not to write it down. Nobody argued with her. This is a standing refusal and may not be argued out of her, and the reason she gave is Volume 03's door.**

**SATURDAY 11 OCTOBER 2014, 22:00 — twelve days after the volume closes, and the volume's last image, and it was never staged.** A six-day term read then begins at 07:00 on Sunday 12 October, one hour after the money stops. Four people could read that term, all four have given a correct answer, none has given an answer, and there is no rule for two of them. **Jonas Mercer and Tomas Renn will not discuss it, and that rule between them is the only instrument either of them produced in the year that is any use at all. It may be named on a wall, in a minute or on a list, by anybody. It may not be staged again and it may not be resolved in Volume 03 by anybody who was not in that corridor.**

## 2. What Volume 02 won, and what it did not

- **Priority access is invalid in a published determination where it was bought with a fee and by nothing else.** One sentence, one page, a sub-substitution of Schedule 4, limited to precedence, with the class of node named and no consequence provided for.
- **NOTHING WAS DISSOLVED. CivicCore keeps the physical network**, the hinges, the floor at Gantry Ward and every contract. It loses the thing it was selling. **The cost of the narrowness is Rook's, delivered in a doorway: a thing that was going to happen anyway now has to be argued for, in public, repeatedly, by people who are not paid to argue, and a clause that cannot be challenged by a person cannot require them to.**
- **A cooperative steward board exists.** Nine seats. Eight filled by people already doing a thing and asked nothing; one filled in a hall by a woman in an apron who stated her price out loud and did not hide a word of it. **It has no money, no chair, one power and no way to lose it, and the ninth of them is a person.**
- **A reading rule**, the Committee's first act in four months that was not a form: where a party relies on either of the two entries for Meter Room 3, the other is to be read with it. **The two entries still stand and still cannot be amended and the Committee was not misled; it was read to, twice.**
- **A record of shapes with no name box on it, by decision and not by oversight**, and the blank line at the bottom of the register of narrowings is still blank and the register has not grown since 4 August and is now a record of a period.

## 3. The new standing instruments, and they are all still unpaid

- **THE FOURTH PAGE** — one week, one place, one number, what was counted, one sentence saying what it is not, no index, no name box, nothing may be added, **and a person may refuse to be counted and the count then says so and says how many refused.** On a public board. Nobody has asked Nina Calder what it is for and she has not said.
- **NO FIFTH PAGE WAS WRITTEN AND SHE REFUSED IN A PUBLIC ROOM AT A COUNTER ON 1 SEPTEMBER**, in the right order, unargued: *the fifth page is the form, the form is a scheme, a scheme is a list with a future in it, and a form is the last thing a person writes and the first thing an institution keeps.* Standing refusal. Nobody may come back to it.
- **A YEAR ON A PIECE OF PAPER IN A POST BOX** — one letter, 1938, thirty rooms, a stopcock that turns, a tag, four meters, one name at the bottom, no household in it at all, and the wrongness named: it is written by a man who is not authorised to write it for a woman who has not been asked what she wants in it.
- **A CONDITION THAT A CONSENT MAY BE GIVEN TWICE** and that the record of the second is not evidence about the first, printed on an enamel strip in a corridor, unsigned, undated and unattributed.
- **A CONDITION THAT A BODY WHICH CANNOT BE REQUIRED TO DO ANYTHING CANNOT BE ASKED WHAT IT WILL DO**, printed on a card beside the register of narrowings, initialled by a records supervisor because a condition nobody has claimed can be put in an edition.

## 4. The new bodies, and the old ones, and the cost of each

- **Nine seats: eight named people and one woman.** Y. Amoah, 41, ambulance duty officer, eleven years. I. Sesay, 51, caretaker, Tarn Street school. D. Fahey, facilities supervisor, Saint Orra west, six years. I. Tarrant, car park supervisor, eleven years. S. Umeh, 58, market porter and lock-up keeper, thirty years. K. Ferris, 33, night bus controller, nine years. W. Crabbe, 62, linen supervisor, nineteen years. G. Nye, 44, self-employed heating engineer, twenty-one years. **And Y. Fofana, 54, named steward of Wren Court, findable at an address, unpaid, the only seat in the body that cannot be emptied by anybody else and therefore the only seat that can empty it, and she does not know that, and is not going to be told, because a person may not be required even to be told.**
- **The only refusal to sit** was Ronan Vasquez, 48, estates, on Friday 22 August 2014, ten days before the eight were seated, in a piece of paper in his own words, **and he did not read section 11 and nobody has told him what is in it** — his reason is the sentence he put on a supplier's acceptance seven days earlier, *it is not a service, it is a person reading a strip, and if anybody makes this a rota I will stop and they can have the paragraph number instead* — and he signed the decontamination verification on 15 August for the same reason, and who says his name goes on a log and does not go on a board and that both of those are true and neither cancels the other. **He is named on a public sheet in the vestibule of the Old Ward. Do not turn him into a man who reads regulations before he refuses; he does not, and the joke of the borough is that he refuses better for it.**
- **Wren Court, thirty-one rooms, no civic address, WAS NOT SEATED. The room tried.** They are right and it has not helped them since 4 July, and being right about it is now printed on a board in a corridor.
- **Saint Orra west is unchanged.** 11:00–13:30 gap, published, unfilled, since 2 April. Bram Osei's refused Thursday in his own words. Priya Ranganathan's card. Terms six days. The gate on its local term. The corridor board has no new name on it.
- **A service manager is still waiting to be told whether a rota is coming, and the only nine people in this city who know are the nine who were in the room, and nobody who does the practice has been told, and that is deliberate, and it is still the worst correct thing anybody in this city has done.**

## 5. Forbidden in Volume 03 and in everything after it, in addition to everything already forbidden

- **Do not open the sealed envelope in the drawer on a lower floor and do not write into it.** One card, about a stair, dated and numbered in biro. Nobody may open it.
- **Do not resolve the fourth page of section 11 to the ninth party.** Nobody acts on it, and it is a lever.
- **Do not let the ninth seat be a chair, a veto, a job, a paid office, a rota, or anything anybody can be required to do.** And do not have the eight learn what they are, and do not have anyone notice what the ninth can do.
- **Do not make the board work.** Its one power is to require a party to answer. A district that cannot pay has a seat and a person in it and a price of £11,280 a year that nobody has asked anybody to pay, and that is the shape of Volume 03.
- **Do not turn the letter into a resolution.** A managing agent's clerk will answer it in about eleven days and nobody in this city is entitled to know what she said.
- **Do not let anybody thank a person for refusing something.** Chapter 90 spent that. Six refusals stand and two more were added in this batch, and none of the eight may be argued out.
- **Do not manufacture a count of the nodes with nobody answering for them.** It has been refused twice, in July and in August, and the record that exists is a record of shapes.
- **Nine hundred and forty is a number of times. Fourteen is a difference between two public numbers and is on no instrument. They were never in the same sentence and must not be.**
- **The old continuity schedule is still in force and still has nobody auditing it, and the drawing layer still has no delete operation, and a chalk board in Gantry Ward still has a nine-millimetre ghost on it that says TIDEGLASS 3.**
- **Volume 03 enters Lowglass as a person with claims and not as a population on a form.** The Weir is on no layer, is not drawn, cannot be seated, is not in the board's eight districts, and is on nobody's list. **A priced-access case is not a recognition case. A node with no term in force is not a person with no address. A duty is an act and not a place.**

# VOLUME 03 OPEN THREADS — as they stand after Chapter 110, Wednesday 3 December 2014

**Read with the "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0001" block at the end of `state/continuity.md`, which carries the canon. This file carries only what is still open and what it is forbidden to do.**

## 1. The nine people and two chairs

- **SEVEN OF NINE. The seventh chair has been empty since Wednesday 8 October 2014, when Kathleen Ferris, 33, night bus controller, wrote four lines on the back of a bus timetable and gave them to the woman who keeps the minutes. Nobody has read them out and nobody may.** The board could not fill it, because a body that cannot require anybody cannot require one of its own, and because six weeks of working out who to want instead is a rota.
- **The eighth chair has been empty since the evening of Wednesday 26 November 2014**, when Stanley Umeh, 58, market porter and lock-up keeper, stood up and said *Right. I'm going* and nobody argued and nobody asked him a second question. **He answered for the shutter and the padlock and what the padlock is for, and the minute records the seat as a lock-up and not as a district or a facility, and the box in the form is left empty on purpose and the minute says so.** A shape where a person was, and the shape is the only interesting thing on the page.
- **Nobody can fill either and nobody can unfill either and there is no power in this city that could.** The body does not dissolve; a dissolution needs six.
- **Four of the nine have said they will stop if it turns into a job.** Gloria Nye said it in eleven words on 8 October and apologised and has not withdrawn it. Ines Tarrant said she meant it and has not stopped and asked to be asked why and nobody was able to ask her. The ninth seat said it in about nine seconds in a records office in September and gave the arithmetic.
- **The ninth seat is a woman of fifty-four who is findable at a shopfront, unpaid, doing it for nothing since 4 July, who has said she will not be paid until she is sixty-one and that after that she will not be, and who is the only door in a building of thirty-one rented rooms.** **Nobody on that board knows that the ninth seat is the only seat that cannot be emptied by anybody else, and therefore the only seat that can empty the body, and she has said out loud twice that she will not be told, and she is right.** Three people know: a clerk who copied it out by hand, a registrar who read it twice, and a records supervisor who read it once and told nobody and gave a reason in a public room. **Do not resolve it. Do not have the board notice it. Do not have anybody tell her.**
- **Page four of section 11 is a dissolution clause at six vacancies, read by three people and told to nobody. Nobody has come near acting on it and nobody may.**

## 2. The letter, and the thing it cost

- **It worked. The four meters are not going into the Block C cupboard; they went to a block of 1974. The freeholder's standing instruction of 14 April was withdrawn. A condition survey of the riser space and the roof void is coming in the spring, on a sheet of A4 in a plastic wallet, and it says *feasibility*, and feasibility has never once in this city produced a restored anything.**
- **Nobody is entitled to know what the managing agent's clerk said and the batch did not find out and Volume 03 must not.**
- **One of the two survey nodes went into the register of narrowings on 14 November and the other did not, and there is nobody anybody is entitled to ask which of them is correct.**
- **IVY MARSDEN, 72, was told on Thursday 13 November by the man who wrote the letter, at her own door, in daylight, with nine minutes' notice, and she refused a copy, and she worked out what the letter was for in about four minutes, and she told him the price of it: the day a piece of paper says the buckets are the block's, the block is a thing that can be asked, and a thing that can be asked is a thing that has a landlord, and a thing that has a landlord is a thing that has a list.** She is no more findable and a great deal more frightened. She let him write down that his name is now in a thing in her head that has to do with her. **Nobody goes up those stairs again.**
- **The two conditions on the Old Ward board stay unreconciled: the city's own paper, printed 14/8, initialled N.C., and sixteen inches below it an enamel strip, unsigned, undated, unattributed, with two initials and one word in pencil on the back of it and the word is *not mine*. The initials are not a signature to the condition and the writer of any batch must never decide in prose whose they are.**

## 3. The two records, and the reading rule

- **Record one, September: a record of shapes, a number, sixteen, no name box by decision.** It cannot be wrong, therefore it could not be found wrong, and that is why it was a photograph.
- **Record two, published the third week of November: FIFTY-ONE NODES on the continuity schedule as at 18:00 on Monday 6 October 2014, THIRTY-EIGHT PARTIES, THIRTEEN SHAPES, checkable at a counter by anybody, for nothing, by post, as of a date. It went out UNSIGNED, with no name box, and about four hundred people have added their names to it.** It establishes nothing and it can be argued with and being arguable is the point.
- **Nobody is required to be told they are in it, and that is the design and not an oversight, and it was said in the room where it was agreed and it may not be softened.**
- **The difference between the two records is FOUR, and Calder can say what three of them are and will not say the fourth, and Jonas Mercer read three and refused the fourth in a room with three people in it, and neither of them will ever write it down.**
- **The fourth of the four is a meter room a person could walk to, and it can be walked to, and the woman who wrote the page thought about that in a corridor on 19 November with her coat on. Unresolved and the most dangerous sentence anybody has produced this year.**
- **The reading rule of 21 August has left the only room it was made in, once, in one copy, on foot, in a woman's inside pocket. It is an act and not a document: a person reading two sentences out loud to another person in a room with the second person able to say no. It cannot be put in a form, a job description, a rota, an establishment or an edition, because any of those turns it into a service. A managing agent has refused a copy of it for a correct reason and the reason may not be argued with. A clerk has announced that she is going to misuse it in a corridor, on her own time, for a person nobody may be told about, and nobody may ask her whether she has done it.**

## 4. The register, and the shape on the wall

- **NINETEEN entries. Three times in nine weeks, every one of the three correct, and a records supervisor has said the true thing about it: a register that grows when nobody is hurt is a register with a motive in it and a register somebody can hold over you, and if anybody ever uses that board against anybody they will not use the meter room, they will use the bulb.**
- **The blank line at the bottom is blank and stays blank and is now the only fixed point in the building.** It was blank on 1 December and it is expected to be blank on 4 January.
- **A form with no name box on it has been used to find a building twice, by careful people doing exactly what it was designed to do, and there is nobody in the whole of this to blame except a man with a register who asked for a state and got one.** A design is not a promise.

## 5. The six words, and the two men they connect

- **`6-L/14. NON-CIVIC PLANT.` Said out loud twice in rooms: one word on 28 October to about eleven people, and nineteen seconds on 21 November to about ten people, including the name of the man who lodged it and did not read it. Both minutes are public and neither contains his name, because there is no box for one.**
- **PETER NAYLOR, 44, level-three acceptance officer, field services, Gantry Ward, out of estates since 2011: signed an acceptance in about ninety seconds in August, does not know what any of the words mean, has a fortnight somewhere in this city with a bucket under a tap, and NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH IS ABLE TO ASK HIM ONE QUESTION ABOUT ANY OF IT. Two people told Jonas Mercer not to and he has not. His name and the six words are now connectable in a public record and he has not been told and cannot be told.**
- **ROYDEN ACHEBE, 49, NDC field services manager: named publicly for the first time on 21 November, in a minuted room, in a sentence about what he did and did not read before lodging a classification on 22 March. He was not in the room, he has not been told, he could be asked about it tomorrow morning, and nobody has asked him. He is NOT the man of forty-four and the two must never be conflated in prose.** He telephoned the shop on 14 August about a Tuesday and Jonas Mercer was the one who answered, and Jonas Mercer has never rung him since either.
- **The six words took four tonnes of stone and about £1,180 off anybody's schedule and put them on nobody's invoice, and they took a lowland piece of ground out of the review, and the street is still on it because the other half of that morning was voted down, and no document anywhere says how the two halves are related.**

## 6. The lowland, and the six shapes

- **The lowland is not on the layer. The layer draws nine service districts, eight named, the ninth not drawn. So the lowland is not in the board's eight districts, so there is no seat for it, and there is no instrument in this city by which there could be one, and the reason is not an oversight and may not be fixed in Volume 03.**
- **Six water points at Tier 1, £1,880 a year, £11,280, in paragraph 6.1 of the minute of 19 May, recorded as received and not as evidence, and nobody has been asked for any part of it, and the chair said in the margin in the clerk's hand: *I will not require a person at six water points. Noted. The reason nobody has asked is the reason this Committee cannot answer.***
- **Six shapes were identified in discussion in a hall on 28 October and are named in no document. One of them is a green pillar with a bucket on a chain and a forty-slot board at the top of a three-hundred-metre street with a canal on one side and boat sheds on the other, whose trap is gone and whose bank is gone, and the ninth seat named it and would not say where it is. The Weir's three positions stand on a kitchen wall with a name against each and a fourth line has a ring of pencil round a blank space. Margo Doré's advance refusal of a pump is unargued, no pump was offered, and none exists. Jonas Mercer did not go up the path in this batch.**
- **The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question and the day after, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause and the Selah Mora log are all unresolved and all forbidden to resolve.**

## 7. The midpoint, and what it cost

- **A letter went through the door of a Methodist hall on Friday 28 November 2014, addressed to a cooperative steward board, care of a hall, because a published minute says where they sit. One side of A4, biro, no crest, no form number, no reference, no date in a box, in a hand that is not a clerk's. It is signed.**
- **Its author is BERNICE OFORI, 34, who has been at a stand at the bottom of Quarry Street at twenty to seven every morning with a folding trolley, and who has kept to a date on that stand with no person on it for eleven years, and who gave no name to anybody in this account until 3 December.** She asked whether the stand is one of the six. She asked for nothing. She signed it on purpose, because a room is not a record, and she said so twice.
- **On Wednesday 3 December at 18:41 a man said her name out loud in a room with about eleven people in it. He did not write it down. Nobody asked him a second question and nobody asked whether it was correct. The minute says a name was stated, once, and does not contain it, and records that the letter was not entered in it and that there is no box for one and that this is a decision and not an oversight. The minute went out on Friday 5 December and is a public document and the name is not in it.**
- **THE NAME IS IN ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE AND IN NO DOCUMENT. There is no instrument in this borough that can un-say a thing in a room, and there is one that can un-write it, and it is the same instrument they have had since March.**
- **BATCH 0002 MUST NOT: put that name in a form, a minute, a return, a holder field or any document; send anybody to that street; have her rescued, helped, given water, given a seat or given anything; have her thank anybody; or resolve what her standing now is. Findability is not answerability, and a person is not a door because a man said her name once.**

## 8. The winter, and the dates that are not written down

- **The Saint Orra west term expires at 06:00 on SUNDAY 4 JANUARY 2015. The terms are six days. The rota has still not been rebuilt. The 11:00–13:30 gap is still published and still unfilled. Bram Osei's refused Thursday is still on the board in his own words and Priya Ranganathan's card is still on the board because she put it there. The gate runs on its local term and cannot be renewed by Jonas, the department or a manager — only by the people who read it aloud at ten at night.**
- **The handover is on the Saturday night before it, which is Saturday 3 January 2015, and NO DATE HAS BEEN WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE FOR THAT SATURDAY NIGHT, because a person is not required to be told and there is nobody in this borough who is required to write it.**
- **The autumn compliance report is out or due with a paragraph number on it for a decontamination verification that was not evidenced for three months, at a hospital with four hundred and twenty women in it. The going-forward verification was weekly and by a named person who is not on a rota, and after October it lapsed, because a man of forty-eight said out loud in a corridor in August that a job in November has a department in it. Nobody has rung him.**
- **A second tray is on a shelf in a building in Gantry Ward with a rubber band and a date in biro and one question outstanding: is there any party who may consent to the works. The answer is no and it has been no since 29 May, and the man who could answer it signed a piece of paper in ninety seconds in August saying *not released* and does not know what any of the words mean.**
- **The claims box is closed and empty. The sealed envelope is in a drawer on a lower floor with one card in it about a stair. Nothing may be written into it and nobody may open it, and a man stood six feet from it in August and did not.**

## 9. The refusals, all ten, none argued out

The mortuary porter's four words in nine seconds · Marguerite Oyelaran's nine seconds and a person-box · Emeka Rusk's, and helping does not go on a form · Margo Doré's advance refusal of a pump · Sanaa Iqbal's twenty minutes a week · Ivo Bamba's, spent in Chapter 90 and not reused · Nina Calder's fifth page · Yolanda Fofana's refusal to answer the day after · **new, and standing: Frances Tolley's, in a corridor, on 26 November** · **new, and standing: Bernice Ofori's, which is the reason the volume has a midpoint.**

**Nobody refuses to be thanked anywhere in Volume 03. Nobody thanks Ivo Bamba or Yvonne Amoah. No minute records that a person has not been thanked, and the ninth seat is not thanked for anything.**

## 10. Still reserved, and forbidden to any batch in this volume

No root reading, no root clause, no succession, no Iona, no Evan, no counter-amendment, no second signature, no Quiet Cut, no origin of the community-watch term, no three-line brass tokens. **Volumes 07, 09 and 15.** **Prue Calloway's location-of-record finding of 23 April — *a building has an address, it is the people who have no location of record, and a panel cannot weigh a claim from a person it cannot locate* — is the Volume 03 door and it has not been opened. It may be opened once, in a room, in daylight, and not resolved.** The forty minutes of 15 April is time until the family knew; **no 48-minute figure may ever be printed.** The forty *hours* of 3 April is the gate being open and is a different number about a different thing. **Nine hundred and forty is a number of times. Fourteen is a difference between two public numbers and is on no instrument. They have never been in the same sentence and must not be, and nine hundred and eighty-six is a number of Wednesdays and must never be in one either.**

# VOLUME 03 OPEN THREADS — as they stand after Chapter 120, Wednesday 28 January 2015

**Read with the "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 111–120" block at the end of `state/continuity.md`, which carries the canon. This file carries only what is still open and what it is forbidden to do.**

## 1. The nine people and two chairs

- **SEVEN OF NINE, unchanged for the whole of the batch.** The seventh chair empty since Wednesday 8 October 2014 (Kathleen Ferris, 33, night bus controller, four lines on the back of a bus timetable that nobody has read out and nobody may). The eighth chair empty since the evening of Wednesday 26 November 2014 (Stanley Umeh, 58, market porter, *right, I'm going*, not asked a second question). **Nobody can fill either and nobody can unfill either and there is no power in this city that could.** It was stated again in a room on 28 January: two chairs is a Tuesday.
- **THERE IS NO CHAIR OF THAT BODY AND THERE NEVER WAS. A body of nine seats has one power — require a party holding continuity to answer, receive the answer, be asked to consider a matter — and a chair is not one of the three parts of it. The ninth seat has gone through the order of things since 4 July because she is the only one of the nine anybody can write to, and she has said in a room that that is not a chair and nobody is to make it into one. Any later batch that gives that body a chair is wrong.**
- Four of the nine have said they will stop if it turns into a job. **On 28 January the ninth seat asked Ines Tarrant, 44, car park supervisor, eleven years, why she meant it, because she had asked on 26 November to be asked and nobody had been able to ask her. She answered in about nine seconds and it is the only reason any of the nine has ever given: if this turns into a job then the nearest person to the barrier is a woman with a job on a board, and in about four years somebody will use that to tell her what the barrier is for. She has not stopped and nothing followed from it and it was not minuted at her request.** Unresolved.
- The ninth seat is findable at a shopfront, unpaid, doing it for nothing since 4 July, **the only door in a building of thirty-one rented rooms, and nobody has told her and nobody is going to.** Do not resolve it. Do not have the board notice it. Do not tell her.
- Page four of section 11 is a dissolution clause at six vacancies, read by **exactly three people and told to nobody: FRANCES TOLLEY, OREN PELL, NINA CALDER.** **On 28 January Jonas Mercer said out loud in a room of nine, in daylight, that he is NOT one of the three and is not going to say who the three are, and said he is not going to say what is on it in a room, and asked for that to be minuted as a decision and not as a virtue. HE HAS NEVER READ IT. HE DID NOT SAY WHAT IS ON IT AND THE NINTH SEAT WAS IN THE ROOM AND WAS NOT TOLD ANYTHING.** It may not be disclosed in Volume 03.

## 2. The letter, and the thing it cost

- It worked. The four meters went to a block of 1974. A condition survey with the word *feasibility* on it is coming in the spring on a sheet of A4 in a plastic wallet.
- **Ivy Marsden, 72, room 14, was told on 13 November by the wrong man and is no more findable and a great deal more frightened, and nobody has gone up those stairs since 14 November, and there is no reason anybody would recognise.** The two conditions on the Old Ward board stay unreconciled; the initials on the back of the enamel strip are never decided in prose.
- **One of the two Carrow Lane survey nodes went into the register of narrowings on 14 November and the other, the roof void, is still on a schedule at £1,880 a year, and there is nobody anybody is entitled to ask which of them is correct.** Unresolved.

## 3. The two records, and the reading rule

- Record one, September: shapes, sixteen, no name box by decision, and it cannot be wrong and therefore could not be found wrong. Record two, November: **fifty-one nodes, thirty-eight parties, thirteen shapes, as at 18:00 on Monday 6 October 2014, checkable at a counter by anybody for nothing by post as of a date, UNSIGNED, no name box, about four hundred people have added their names, and about eleven have obtained the extract and two have written back and been wrong and the clerk wrote *enclosure received, no comment* and that is in the file.**
- The difference between the two records is **four**. Calder can say what three are and will not say the fourth; Jonas Mercer read three and refused the fourth in a room and will not write it down. **Unresolved. Do not resolve.**
- **THE READING RULE HAS NOW BEEN USED ONCE, IN A CORRIDOR, AT A COUNTER, IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS, BY FRANCES TOLLEY, TO PETER NAYLOR, 44, ON AN UNDATED DAY IN JANUARY 2015, AND SHE IS NOT GOING TO BE ASKED WHETHER IT WORKED, HE IS NOT GOING TO BE TOLD HE WAS IN IT, AND JONAS MERCER FOUND OUT BECAUSE THE MAN SAID FOUR WORDS AT THE COUNTER ON 22 JANUARY: `I was read to.`** It established nothing, it can be argued with, and being arguable is the point. **Nobody may ask Frances Tolley whether she has done it, and nobody may ask Peter Naylor one question about any of it, ever.** The one copy is still in her inside pocket. Beverley Annis has still refused a copy for a correct reason.

## 4. The register, and the seventh refusal

- **NINETEEN ENTRIES, unmoved between 26 November 2014 and 28 January 2015. The blank line at the bottom is blank and is the only fixed point in the building.** Entry 19 is a bulb and the bulb is out.
- **A REASON COLUMN WAS ASKED FOR IN WRITING ON 2 JANUARY 2015 BY M. FURNEAUX, 61, OF THE REAR BLOCK, CARROW LANE, AND WAS REFUSED BY NINA CALDER AT A COUNTER IN THE RIGHT ORDER IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS, AND IT IS THE SEVENTH REFUSAL, AND IT STANDS, AND NOBODY MAY COME BACK TO IT. A REASON COLUMN IS A SENTENCE COLUMN AND A SENTENCE COLUMN IS A NAME BOX WITH THE SERIAL NUMBERS LEFT OFF. The letter went back with nothing in it on 5 January. He will write again and she will say no again in the same words and it will not be the eighth of anything, because it is not a list.**
- A form with no name box on it has now been used to find a building three times, all of them by careful people doing exactly what it was designed to do, and there is nobody to blame except a man with a register who asked for a state and got one. A design is not a promise.

## 5. The six words, and the three men

- **`6-L/14. NON-CIVIC PLANT.` Said out loud twice in rooms, in October and November. Both minutes are public and neither contains his name, because there is no box for one.**
- **PETER NAYLOR, 44, level-three acceptance officer, field services, Gantry Ward, out of estates since 2011.** Signed an acceptance in about ninety seconds in August, does not know what any of the words mean, **was read two sentences in a corridor in January 2015 and was able to say no and did not, and does not know he was in it. NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH IS ABLE TO ASK HIM ONE QUESTION ABOUT ANY OF IT, EVER, AND HE IS A WALL.**
- **ROYDEN ACHEBE, 49, NDC field services manager.** Named publicly on 21 November in a room he was not in. **STILL NOT TOLD. Jonas Mercer did not go, did not telephone, did not use the switchboard, did not go to the team, did not go to the building, and did not ask anybody. HE IS A DOOR, AND A DOOR IS NOT A FAVOUR, AND A MAN WHO CAN BE ASKED ONE QUESTION ABOUT ANYTHING FOR EVER BY ANYBODY IS NOT FINDABLE, HE IS AVAILABLE. The two must never be conflated in prose, in a document or in a narration.**
- **AND A THIRD MAN, 48, who did the weekly verification for about eleven weeks in the autumn with no name against it, stopped in October because a job in November has a department in it, said it out loud in a corridor in August to about four people, and HAS NOT BEEN ASKED ABOUT IT SINCE AND HAS NOT BEEN RUNG.** He is not a wall and not a door. He is a man who said a correct thing in a corridor and was not asked about it.
- The six words took four tonnes of stone and about £1,180 off anybody's schedule and put them on nobody's invoice. Unresolved.

## 6. The lowland, and the six shapes

- **The lowland is not on the layer, is not in the board's eight districts, cannot be seated, and has no seat. THE REASON IS NOT THAT IT CANNOT PAY: a district that cannot pay has a seat and a person in it and has not thereby lost its vote. The reason is that a node with no party is not a party, and a place is not a person, and the registrar said on 13 January that the reason is correct, has been correct since 1 March, and is not going to be fixed. THAT REASON MAY NOT BE FIXED IN VOLUME 03.**
- **Six water points at Tier 1, £11,280 a year, are in paragraph 6.1 of the minute of 19 May, recorded as received and not as evidence, and nobody has been asked to pay any part of it. In January the figure is a heating figure and Gloria Nye did the conversion out loud: it is one boiler. The six are not on the layer and therefore not in the eight districts.**
- **ABU BAKAYOKO, 58, twenty-six years at a stand at the top of the lowland market, ASKED TO BE COUNTED on 13 January 2015 in a room in daylight, and what he wanted was not a seat and not money and not a number: a line in a minute saying that a thing exists at a place where a thing is needed. He gave the state of five of the six in about two minutes. HE REFUSED THE SIXTH OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED HIM, AND THE REFUSAL IS MINUTED AND NOBODY MAY ASK HIM WHERE IT IS.** The sixth shape is the Weir and will not be named in this volume in any document that leaves the street.
- **NO SEVENTH SHAPE WAS ADDED TO ANY RECORD. The reason was given out loud in seventeen words on 13 January: a shape has to have a place you can point at, and that has a person in it. THE SEVENTH IS NOT RESOLVED.** One Lattice notice in the batch, in Chapter 0116: the Tier 1 schedule line for six nodes, £11,280, term to 06:00 Sunday 7 June 2015, no party answering, may not be matched against any list of addresses.
- The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question and the day after, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause and the Selah Mora log are all unresolved and all forbidden to resolve. **Margo Doré has not been asked anything since 1 June, is not angry and has not forgiven him, and nobody has gone up that path. A pump was not offered and none was, and there are pumps for sale in four shops at about £240 and none of them has ever been in a van on that street.**

## 7. The name in eleven people

- **BERNICE OFORI, 34, IS A NAME IN ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE IN A METHODIST HALL IN TIDEGLASS AND IN NO DOCUMENT, and there is no list of who has it and there is not going to be one, and nobody is to go down there, and nobody has.**
- A name said in a room cannot be un-said, produced, checked, argued with, minuted, published, attached to a claim, or found by anybody who is not one of the eleven. In about four years somebody is going to come with a piece of paper about that stand and there is not going to be one thing anybody in this borough can put beside it, and whether that is the best outcome available or the worst is not going to be said this year.
- **A PRINTED SLIP FROM COMMITTEE SERVICES CAME TO THE HALL ON 19 JANUARY 2015 WITH THREE BOXES AND A LINE FOR A NAME, and the third box is the only correct answer on the sheet and there is nowhere on the sheet to put it, and NOBODY IN THAT HALL SENT IT. The minute-taker wrote a second, unsigned, handwritten note beside it, in about four minutes, with four lines of her own biro on it and nothing at the top of it. On 21 January, in daylight, in an empty hall, she came through the door she wrote through on 28 November, read both, put no mark in any box, and DID NOT WRITE ON THE PRINTED ONE — a sheet with a crest and a form number is a document and her name is not going on one. She wrote on the second one, which cannot be used to show anybody anything: *I have not withdrawn anything. I asked a question. There is no sitting to attend and nobody has asked me to attend one.*** She left both pieces on the table and took nothing with her, because her own November letter was still in a handbag in a kitchen four hundred yards away.
- **THE TWO PIECES OF PAPER WITH HER NAME ON THEM ARE HER LETTER OF 28 NOVEMBER, IN A HANDBAG IN A KITCHEN OFF A LANDING, AND HER OWN FOUR LINES, WRITTEN AT THE TOP OF A SHEET OF A4 THAT ALREADY HAD FOUR LINES OF THE MINUTE-TAKER'S BIRO ON IT, IN A PLASTIC WALLET IN THE DRAWER OF THE TRESTLE TABLE IN THAT HALL, AND THE DRAWER HAS NO KEY, AND THERE IS NO LIST OF WHAT IS IN ANY DRAWER IN THAT BUILDING. NEITHER IS A DOCUMENT AND NEITHER MAY BE OPENED.** **HER NAME IS NOT ON THE PRINTED SLIP AND MAY NOT BE PUT ON IT.**
- **BATCH 0003 MUST NOT: put that name in a form, in a minute, in a return, in a holder field or in any document; send anybody down there; have her rescued, helped, given water, given a seat or given anything; have her thank anybody; or resolve what her standing now is. Findability is not answerability and a person is not a door because a man said her name once.**

## 8. The winter, and the dates that are not written down

- **The Saint Orra west term expired at 06:00 on Sunday 4 January 2015 and the gate opened at seven, and it opened because of the handover on Saturday 3 January at ten o'clock and for no other reason. It was read by EZRA MBEKI and TREVOR NUNN, 58, a night porter, in a waiting area with four chairs and a machine for hot water that has not worked since August. NOTHING WAS WRITTEN DOWN AND NOTHING IS GOING TO BE, and that is not an oversight, and it is the first time in this account that a thing has been got right by nobody writing it down, and nobody in this borough is going to be told that it worked.**
- The 11:00–13:30 gap is still published and unfilled and has been since 2 April. Bram Osei's refused Thursday is still on the board in his own words with *no reason I can give* under a *no reason given* in somebody else's hand. Priya Ranganathan's card is still there because she put it there. **The rota has still not been rebuilt.**
- **THE MAN OF FORTY-EIGHT WHO DID THE WEEKLY VERIFICATION STOPPED IN OCTOBER AND HAS NOT BEEN ASKED AND HAS NOT BEEN RUNG, and the autumn compliance report is out with a paragraph number on it in front of four hundred and twenty women.**
- A second tray is on a shelf in a building in Gantry Ward with a rubber band and a date in biro and one question outstanding: is there any party who may consent to the works. The answer is no and has been no since 29 May.
- The claims box is closed and empty. The sealed envelope is in a drawer on a lower floor and may not be opened or written into.
- **TERMS ARE SIX DAYS AND THE NEXT EXPIRY IS 06:00 ON SUNDAY 1 FEBRUARY 2015, read at 22:00 on Saturday 24 January. A TERM IS READ ON THE SATURDAY NIGHT BEFORE THE SUNDAY IT STARTS ON, SO ONE SATURDAY NIGHT IS A WEEK AFTER THE SATURDAY NIGHT BEFORE IT: 31 JANUARY, 7 FEBRUARY, 14 FEBRUARY, 21 FEBRUARY, 28 FEBRUARY, AND 7 MARCH 2015, WHICH IS THE SEVENTH OF THEM IN SIX WEEKS. NEVER DESCRIBE THE TERM ITSELF AS SEVEN DAYS — IT IS SIX DAYS, AND THE SATURDAY NIGHTS ARE WHAT ARE A WEEK APART. THERE IS NO DATE WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE FOR THAT SATURDAY NIGHT, OR FOR ANY OF THE SATURDAY NIGHTS BEFORE IT, because a person is not required to be told and there is nobody in this borough who is required to write it.**
