# VOLUME 05 OPEN THREADS — after Continuation 0011, Chapter 304, Friday 10 March 2017

**Read with the "VOLUME 05, CONTINUATION 0011 — CHAPTERS 295–304" block at the end of `state/continuity.md`, which carries the canon for these ten chapters and supersedes this block wherever the two differ, and with the "VOLUME 05, CONTINUATION 0010 — CHAPTERS 285–294" block above it, which still governs everything those ten chapters did. **VOLUME 05 IS NOT CLOSED AND IS NOT DECLARED CLOSED. THE SERIES IS AT 304 OF 720. NOTHING IN CONTINUATION 0011 RESOLVED ANYTHING AND NOTHING IN IT MAY BE UNDONE, IMPROVED ON, RESTATED OR ANSWERED. EVERY DAY-COUNT BELOW IS ADDED UP FROM ITS ANCHOR ON THE DAY IN QUESTION AND NOT CARRIED FORWARD FROM A SUMMARY: at Friday 10 March 2017 the blank line under the nineteenth is eight hundred and thirty-five days, the card in the slot is seven hundred and forty-nine, and the letter in the tray is seven hundred and fifty-one.****

## 1. A rail back on four steps

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE THING IS DONE, TWO PEOPLE TALK ABOUT IT IN A DOORWAY, BOTH OF THEM ARE RIGHT, NEITHER ASKS THE OTHER FOR ANYTHING, WHAT FOLLOWS IS NOTHING, AND THE BOROUGH DOES NOT NOTICE.** A handrail off its two brackets since December, lying across four outside steps under a stone. The man in his thirties put it back in about a quarter of an hour on his own time, with two plugs in the four holes the sheared fixings left.
- **THE TWO WHO SPOKE: the woman of about forty-two with the key to that hall, going up at ten past four on her way to open it, and the man who works nights, coming down at half past four for a paper.** He says out loud to nobody that somebody has had that up. Then the two of them stand in the doorway of that flat.
- **HE DID NOT GO AND ASK HER, AND SHE DID NOT ASK HIM.** He notes that the rail had been on the step since about December. He says the only thing to do about it would have been to ring the woman whose kitchen it was, and then says he did it on his own time with his own plugs, so there was nothing to ring anybody about. His reason for not knocking is that a knock would have cost her a cup of tea and cost her head something worse.
- **A PERSON WHO IS NOT COUNTED IS NOT A PERSON WHO IS HELPED, AND NOTHING IN THAT CHAPTER SAYS OTHERWISE: he is on the days and at two in the morning, the rail was never in his wages, he is on nothing, the woman who let him that flat was not told and would not have been interested, and a man who is useful is on nothing either, because the sheet in the van was for a kitchen. THE RAIL STAYED ON THE WALL FOR THE REST OF THE WEEK, THE STONE WAS STILL UNDER THAT DOOR SILL, THE BROKEN BRACKETS WERE IN A SIDE POCKET IN A VAN, AND HE NEVER WENT BACK UP THOSE STEPS AGAIN.**

## 2. A door, an arm, and a piece of timber

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE BUILDING DOES A SECOND THING TO SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT COUNTED, IN A DIFFERENT PLACE, SOMEBODY PUTS SOMETHING WHERE IT WILL NOT HAPPEN AGAIN, AND THE SOMEBODY ELSE IS A MAN WHO IS NOT ON ANYTHING AND WHO SAYS NOTHING, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM WHY AND NOBODY WILL.** A street door at the foot of that outside stair, a closer dead since before anybody can remember, three marks on the plaster opposite. At twenty past two at night it caught the woman of about fifty-five above the wrist. Cold tap over that meter, a tea towel, back up.
- **THE MAN IN HIS SIXTIES CAME IN AT TWENTY-FIVE TO SIX AND DID THREE THINGS IN TWENTY MINUTES**: the arm off that door; a plate on the jamb, out of the cupboard with the fire blanket in it, so the arm can be put back on and made to work; and a length of timber on edge in the corner, wedged with two offcuts, stopping the door a hand's width short of the wall. He tells her the timber is the belt to the braces. He says nothing else and goes out to his car.
- **HER PROBLEM IS NOT SOLVED AND SHE IS NOT HOUSED BY ANYBODY.** Still a room on a month. The hasp on the inside of that door is where it was put in the last week of October; she lifts the handle and gets her elbow past the frame about a hundred times a year and always has. Nobody arranged that door, nobody asked her about it, nobody told her who put the hasp on, and that fitting has never been raised with her. The marks are still on the plaster and there is a fourth beside them.
- **A LADDER IS NOT A CREW, A LADDER IS NOT A RESCUE, A TROLLEY IS NOT AN ARRANGEMENT AND A MAN WITH A TROLLEY IS NOT A CREW, AND NO TROLLEY IS IN THAT CHAPTER.**

## 3. A bath refused and left in a yard

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE THING IS BROUGHT, IT IS REFUSED IN ONE WORD, IT IS PUT DOWN WHERE NEITHER OF THEM AGREED, IT IS NOT ASKED FOR AGAIN, AND THE PERSON WHO REFUSED IT DOES NOT KNOW WHY AND NOBODY ASKS THEM.** A steel bath about five foot long out of a house off the Cleeve road in January; that house had a new one in by mid-month; two car boot sales, no takers; a van with no sign on it at the end of Marsh Lane since mid-month.
- **THE ONE WORD WAS *NO*, AND HE SAID *RIGHT*.** He had come off a bathroom and nobody wanted it. She said it was not for her, that the landing is the way in past her own door, and that a bath across the passage would be gone over in the dark. He asked where to put it. She named two places it could not, then said she did not know where it went and that it was not hers and never had been. **THEY AGREED ON NOTHING, AND IT WENT DOWN THE STAIR AGAIN AND STOPPED ON ITS SIDE UNDER THE PASSAGE WINDOW IN THAT YARD, ACROSS FROM THE CHAIR PUT THERE IN JANUARY.**
- **HE CARRIED IT UP BECAUSE HE HAD A JOB ON A SHOP NEAR THE TOP OF MARSH LANE, HAD TO MOVE THE VAN AT HALF PAST TWO, AND HAD NOBODY TO HELP HIM WITH A HANDCART, AND HE HAD NEVER MET THE WOMAN HE PUT IT DOWN IN FRONT OF.** The man who works nights came in at six, looked at it and went up. The woman who works in a shop saw it through the gate on the Wednesday and went on to the bus stop and has not said where it came from and does not know. Nobody asked the woman of about fifty-five whether she wanted one. He did not bring another and nobody asked him for one.
- **BEING TURNED AWAY IS NOT A RESCUE AND IS NOT A FALLING-OUT, AND A GIFT THAT IS REFUSED IS NOT A PURCHASE. THE TWO OF THEM WERE ENTIRELY CIVIL AND NOTHING WAS SAID THAT ANYBODY WOULD HAVE HAD TO REPEAT.**

## 4. A sheet, two boxes, and a shop's name

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE FOURTH COST ALSO GOES NOWHERE, AND SOMEONE PUTS A NAME ON NOTHING, AND A MAN WHO HAS BEEN ON NOTHING IS NAMED IN A ROOM BY SOMEBODY WHO HAS THE KEY AND HE IS NOT ASKED WHETHER THAT IS ALL RIGHT.** A sheet of A4 came under that hall's door on a Wednesday morning, with a hundred and twenty-four pounds on it against *emergency lighting and external luminaire*, three lines of which the first says the work cannot be done until a named person has given an account in writing on the same date as the work, and a printed line at the foot with a box for a name and a box for an account. A different firm from the other two. The man in his sixties found it on the doormat at half past six, read it standing up in the dark, stood in the passage between that hall and the shop next door for about twenty minutes, and took it home in his pocket.
- **THE ACCOUNTING FIGURES BEFORE THIS ONE, NONE OF THEM RESOLVED AND NONE OF THEM RE-USED: A HUNDRED AND EIGHTY POUNDS FOR A BOILER NOBODY OWNS, TWO HUNDRED AND SIXTY POUNDS FOR A TEST NOBODY ASKED FOR AND NOW IN THE BIN, AND A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIVE POUNDS FOR A SERVICE NOBODY ASKED FOR WHICH IS STILL ON THAT TABLE FACE UP.** The letting agent's card with a mobile number on it is on that table too and has been there since the Wednesday before Christmas.
- **SHE WROTE A SHOP'S NAME IN THE FIRST BOX AND THE SECOND ONE LEFT EMPTY.** The woman of about forty-two with the key to that hall took a chewed pencil off the ledge behind the counter and wrote, in capitals, the name over the door of the shop next door. The man in his forties did not tell her that shop was not hers to answer for, and she had no two-halved sentence ready. The man in his sixties set out what it would do — a light on that shop's account, an invoice to a company with the deeds, a hundred and twenty-four pounds paid by a company that has never been in that room, and that room left with a light and nothing on it — and she said she knew and would not do it again, and he said it was the wrong name and the right idea.
- **A DOCUMENT IS NOT A BODY, A PRICE THAT MOVES IS NOT A PLOT, THE PRICE DID NOT MOVE, AND NOBODY WAS GIVEN AN ACCOUNT OR A STANDING OR A NAME.**
- **AND SHE NAMED HIM.** She told the man in his forties, without turning round, that the man who works nights had been in that yard at about seven on the Tuesday with his coat open, looking at the side of a bath, and had been there since about six. He had his cup in both hands and neither took his coat off nor put it on. The room went on for a minute and a half. Nobody asked her anything about having said it, she did not explain it, he was not asked whether it was all right, and he left at about half past nine, coat on, unlocking the door with a key from a pocket of it.

## 5. The same answer in a wind at a shelter

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE SAME ANSWER COMES BACK, SOMEBODY WHO HEARD IT THE FIRST TIME SAYS SO OUT LOUD IN A STREET, AND THE HALL HEARS THAT IT WENT ROUND A STREET, AND NEITHER FACT HELPS ANYBODY.** The man from the parade said it out loud on the open bit of hard at the top of Marsh Lane at about ten past eight, having come up to see whether a lorry had gone up the other road instead. The man in a fleece going to the works gave back the first answer, which is that it is the register, because that book has a date at the top of every page and a thing somebody did is not a thing somebody wrote down.
- **AND THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP SAID OUT LOUD ON THE PAVEMENT THAT SHE HAD HEARD THAT ONE OFF HIM BEFORE AND HAD NEVER ONCE SEEN ANYBODY ANSWER IT, AND HE SAID HE HAD SAID IT OUTSIDE MORE TIMES THAN SHE HAD SAID IT IN AND THAT IT WAS THE SAME ANSWER AND WAS NOT GOING TO CHANGE FOR BEING SAID TWICE IN TWO PLACES, AND NOBODY ARGUED AND NOBODY CALLED HIM RIGHT.**
- **THE WOMAN WITH A CLIPBOARD WAS SPOKEN TO FOR THE FIRST TIME BY ANYBODY IN THIS ACCOUNT, ON THE SUBJECT OF BUSES.** She said People, both ways, at the door; her round finishes with April and does not carry on past it; she has never counted a bus stop in her life and it seemed a silly thing to be asked for; she had a flask. On the Saturday the easel came down, was leaned against the side of the bank and went round the back, and she was still on that hard.
- **A FAMILIAR WRONG ANSWER IS NOT A CONFIRMED ONE AND NOT A WEAKENED ONE AND NOT A SECOND ONE OF THE SAME. THERE ARE STILL FOUR AND NOT FIVE. THE THIRD WAS NOT CORRECTED, NOT IMPROVED ON AND NOT ADDED TO, THE FOURTH WAS NOT CALLED A FIFTH, THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JUNE WAS NOT PUT BACK TO FOUR, AND THE MAN WHO INVENTED THE FIRST ONE IS NOT IN THAT ROOM, IS NOT TOLD AND MUST NOT BE TOLD.**

## 6. Two things instead of one

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE PERSON WHO FINDS IT WAS LOOKING FOR SOMETHING ELSE AND GETS TWO THINGS INSTEAD OF ONE, THE THING IS PUT SOMEWHERE ELSE, NOBODY IS TOLD, AND THE PERSON WHO LEFT IT IS NEVER ASKED.** the back door of that shop had a barrel in it since the summer, and by then the barrel will be about halfway round, and a locksmith's card had been in her pocket since the second week of November.
- **THE CHEST: about two foot by three, a division down the middle, a rope handle at each end, about four inches of assorted galvanised hinges and about nine loose screws in a tobacco tin. IT HAD BEEN PUT AGAINST THE OUTSIDE OF A SHED WALL IN THE YARD BY THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES IN THE LAST WEEK OF NOVEMBER, UNDER A SHEET OF DPM WEIGHTED WITH BATTEN, AND NOBODY HAD EVER OPENED IT.** The dpm had come off in the wind and was over the alley fence with its tape cut off.
- **THE TWO THINGS WERE THE CHEST AND THE BARREL, AND THE BARREL WAS ON THE SHELF OVER THE DESK IN THE OFFICE ALL ALONG, WITH A BIT OF PENCIL ON THAT SHELF BESIDE IT READING *BACK DOOR* IN A HAND NOT HERS. SHE CHANGED THE LOCK HERSELF, WITH A SCREWDRIVER, INSIDE TEN MINUTES FROM HALF PAST TWELVE. THERE WAS NO TIN OF ODDMENTS AND THERE NEVER HAD BEEN ONE, AND SHE LOOKED ALONG THE SHELF, UNDER THE BENCH AND BEHIND IT FOR IT TWICE BEFORE SHE GAVE UP.**
- **THE CHEST WENT ALONG THE LEFT-HAND WALL OF THE SHED BELOW THE SHELF, DIVIDED END FACING THE DOOR, A MILK CRATE IN FRONT OF IT, AND THAT DOOR DRAWN TO. THE MAN WHO WORKS IN THAT SHOP ON A WEEKDAY WAS NOT THERE THAT SUNDAY, AND THERE WAS NOBODY ELSE IN THE BUILDING TO TELL.**
- **FOUR DAYS LATER THE MAN IN took nine feet of capping into that yard at about ten past ten, AND WENT ROUND THE BACK OF THE SHED LOOKING FOR BATTEN HE KNEW HE HAD PUT THERE HIMSELF, AND THERE WAS NO BATTEN, NO DPM OVER ANYTHING AND NO CRATE, AND SOMEBODY HAD DRIVEN THE LEAF ON THE SHED FLOOR INTO A CORNER WITH A BOOT. HE ASKED AT THE FRONT WHETHER THAT SHOP HAD EVER HAD ANY BATTEN AT THE BACK AND WAS TOLD IT HAD NOT HAD ANYTHING AT THE BACK IN HIS LIFE, AND HE SAID RIGHT and came back a week later with batten of his own, at his own cost.** A thing that is found is not a discovery, and the person who left it has never been asked about anything ever put in that shed.
- **THE DRAWER UNDER THE TRADE COUNTER WAS NOT OPENED, NOT COUNTED, NOT ASKED ABOUT AND IS NOT NAMED IN THAT CHAPTER, AND THE DRAWER UNDER THE VESTIBULE COUNTER IS NOT MENTIONED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH. IN FACT THE WORD *DRAWER* IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS, AND THE WORD *ENVELOPE* IS TOO. A COPY IS NOT A WALL AND AN ENVELOPE IS NOT A TRAY.**

## 7. Nine things said out loud on a pavement

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE LIST IS MADE OUT LOUD AND NONE OF IT IS WRITTEN DOWN, A WOMAN OFFERS HIM ONE THING SHE HAS AND HE DOES NOT TAKE IT AND SHE DOES NOT INSIST, AND THE LIST IS ON NOTHING BY THE END OF THE DAY.** a white van with a fridge standing in it came up the street at half past ten, could not get out again, and reversed. The man in his thirties stood on that pavement with a pad of work notes shut in his left hand and did not open it once in about half an hour.
- **THE NINE: a sign on the corner of the wall where that street ends, because three vans have gone up it in a fortnight; the top of the street, which is not on the gritting round; the pump in the doorway at the bottom, stopcock off since before Christmas on purpose; the step outside the shut shop, down two inches at the outside corner under a piece of blue lino that has come away at one end; the gulls into the top flat's loft through a ventilation tile with polystyrene against it; the last bus at about ten past four; the fact that a man can fall down there and there is nobody to ring, which he has now stopped saying; and two more he gave without stopping.**
- **THE MAN IN A FLEEVE SAID THAT WAS A LIST AND ASKED WHAT THE GOOD OF IT WAS TO ANYBODY, AND WAS TOLD NOTHING, AND THAT HE COULD READ IT. THE WOMAN WITH A SHOPPING BAG OFFERED HIM ABOUT HALF A BAG OF ROCK SALT FROM HER PORCH, A PAPER SACK FROM DECEMBER, AND SAID SHE DID NOT WANT ANYTHING FOR IT AND DID NOT WANT IT IN WRITING.** He said no and said why: taking it would put a woman in it, and in a fortnight she would have to ask what she wanted for it, and there was nothing she could ask for because he could not put it in anywhere. He also said salt will not do for that step, because it works its way down onto ice and comes back up through the stone leaving a white bloom, and in a month that step is a different colour from the one next to it. She said right, went on up the street, and neither returned with the sack nor mentioned the salt again. The jug is still in her porch.
- **A LIST IS NOT A DOCUMENT AND A LIST IS NOT A FORM AND THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS BOROUGH. NOBODY WAS GIVEN A TITLE, A GRADE, A DEPARTMENT, A CHAIR, A SEAT, A MEMBERSHIP, A FORM, A ROW ON A ROTA OR A WAY OF BEING SAID ANYTHING ABOUT. THE PAD WAS NOT OPENED ONCE BETWEEN HALF PAST TEN AND TWENTY TO TWELVE. ON THE THURSDAY, WORKING BESIDE A MAN IN CORRANCE LANE, HE PUT ONE OF THE NINE INTO THE AIR AGAIN; THE MAN ASKED WHICH ONE, AND HE COULD NOT RECALL THE RUN OF THEM, AND HE PUT NOTHING ON PAPER EVEN THEN. A LIST THAT STAYS ON A KNEE IS NOT A LIST ANYBODY KEEPS.**

## 8. A lamp in a porch, and nobody either way

- **THE MAJOR TURN: THE WORK IS DONE EARLY ON A MORNING BY SOMEBODY WHO WAS NEVER ASKED AND WHO ASKED FOR NOTHING, THE BUILDING IS DIFFERENT AFTERWARDS, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO NOTICES IS ONE PERSON WHO WAS NOT THERE WHEN IT HAPPENED.** The man in the hi-vis was at the front of the shop next door at half past six with a set of keys belonging to a man four miles off, because that shop's landlord is in a town sixty miles off and does not come, and he has walked that parade four times since Christmas.
- **HE PUT A LADDER ON THE TOP STEP OF THAT PORCH, HAD THE OLD LAMP OUT IN ABOUT FOUR MINUTES, FOUND A DEAD STUB OF FLEX ABOVE THAT SWITCH, CUT IT OFF, FED THE FLEX BACK UP INTO THE ROSE AND THE SWITCH, PRESSED THE SWITCH WITH HIS THUMB, AND WENT UP THE PARADE TOWARDS THE BANK. THE FITTING HAD BEEN DEAD SINCE ABOUT 2011 AND THAT SWITCH INSIDE HAD NEVER DONE ANYTHING. HE TOLD NOBODY ANYTHING, NOT ONE PERSON, QUOTED NO FIGURE, LEFT NO CARD, WROTE NOTHING DOWN, AND CAME UP THAT PARADE AGAIN ON THE TUESDAY FOR A JOB FOUR DOORS ALONG AND DID NOT GO PAST THAT HALL.**
- **THE ONLY PERSON WHO NOTICED WAS THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP, AT ABOUT TEN PAST EIGHT THAT NIGHT, WHO STOPPED AT THE DOOR.** She reported a light on and a lamp put in that porch. The man in his forties said aye and did not put down what was in his hand, which was a scrap with a figure on it and was not about a lamp. She said right, said she was not going to be the one to ask, and said whoever it was had better have had a ladder, because there is a step on that porch you can go over. **NOBODY IN THAT ROOM, AND NOBODY IN THAT TOWN, PUT IT TO THE MAN WHO PUT IT THERE THAT THE LAMP BELONGS ON THAT PORCH, AND THE WOMAN WITH THE KEY HEARD ALL OF IT AND SAID NOTHING ABOUT IT.**
- **NOBODY MAY GIVE ANYBODY PERMISSION IN THIS CHAPTER, AND THE WORD IS AT ZERO IN IT. A LADDER IS NOT A CREW AND A LADDER IS NOT A RESCUE. A PERSON WHO IS NOT COUNTED IS NOT A PERSON WHO IS HELPED: the man who works nights came up that parade on the Wednesday afterwards, saw a lit porch, and went past it. The lamp is on a dusk sensor and dies at about twenty minutes past nine, and there is a bit of print under the switch giving the type, the wattage and an item number nobody in this borough has had reason to write down.**

## 9. A second question, from a second person

- **THE MAJOR TURN: HE IS ASKED ONE THING BY SOMEBODY WHO HAS HEARD OF HIM AND NOT OF HIM, HE ANSWERS IT, HE GOES HOME, AND THE ONLY CONSEQUENCE IS THAT SOMEBODY ELSE NOW KNOWS THE FIRST PERSON KNOWS HE EXISTS.** The lad of about eighteen is on the dishes and that is the only thing he does.
- **HE WAS IN FIVE ROOMS ON THAT FRIDAY, ON TWO STREETS AND IN THREE BUILDINGS, AND THE FIRST TWO ARE NOT THE SAME ROOM AND NOT THE SAME DOORWAY: the kitchen at the back of a parade from half past seven; the floor of the shop that shuts early on the same parade from half past ten; a kitchen in Corrance Lane at half past one; a corridor on the first floor of a building by the water at ten past three; a hall off a parade at about half past seven in the evening.**
- **TWO THINGS HAPPENED ON THAT DAY. That shop had one chair more than it had on the Tuesday** — the plain wooden one with the ply seat and the ring on the top of the back, standing by the wall, not in the row — **and the woman who runs that shop did not put no question to him about it and did not look at it twice, and he put it on the top of the stack laid as the others are and sitting at their angle, and she looked at the stack for a second and said two hundred years. **AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FIVE SENT A CARRIER BAG WITH A FOLDED BLANKET IN IT AND SOMETHING HEAVY UNDER THE BLANKET TO THE BOTTOM OF THAT STAIR**, and he did not open it and did not ask what was in it, and on the Wednesday it was still at the foot of those stairs with the landing clear.
- **THE ONE QUESTION CAME IN THE HALL FROM THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES, WHO HAD HEARD OF HIM ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF JANUARY AND HAD NEVER BEEN IN A ROOM WITH HIM.** He asked which of the two places he did first and was told that this one is at ten and the Corrance one is at half past one, so this one is first. He said right and picked his cup up. That took about eight seconds. He asked nothing else that evening and nothing about the parade, the corridor, the stairs or the room the corridor ends in. The lad went round that room four times with the tray, and nobody at that table used either of the two words people use for somebody his age, and What the man from the parade has said since the summer stayed unsaid in that room.
- **AND THE ONLY CONSEQUENCE: on his way past the end of the table the lad heard the woman of about forty-two with the key tell the man in his forties that he had asked him something, and he said it was a question about which of two places he does first, and she said aye and that he never asked her anything.** The thing he asked in that room in June was not put to anybody in it again and was not answered by anybody in it. He has no title, grade, department, chair, seat, membership, form or row on a rota, being asked is not a standing, being useful is not a job, and being in a room is not being a member. **NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH CONTAINS A THANKS OF ANY KIND AND THE WORD *THANK* IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN.**

## 10. A borough on a hard Friday

- **THE REGISTER IS AT NINETEEN, THE LINE UNDER IT IS EIGHT HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIVE DAYS AND IS STILL BLANK, AND THAT IS THE ONE DAY-COUNT IN THE PROSE OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** On Friday the tenth a man and a woman carried a box of files into that room and set them on a shelf, and a sleeve caught the register and pushed it out past the edge so that its last page stood open on the air. **THE LAST THING IN IT CONCERNS A YARD LAMP WITH ITS BULB IN A COLUMN HEADED MAINTENANCE, WHICH WENT OUT ABOUT NOVEMBER TWO YEARS AGO AND WAS NEVER CHANGED, AND UNDER THAT ENTRY IS ONLY THE LINE THE PAPER MAKES FOR ITSELF, WHICH STOPS ABOUT TWO INCHES SHORT OF THE BOTTOM EDGE OF A BOOK ABOUT THAT DEEP. NEITHER OF THEM LOOKED AT IT. A WOMAN WHO WAS NOT IN THAT ROOM SET A BINDER AGAINST IT AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST TEN NOT KNOWING WHAT LAY UNDER IT. NOBODY OPENED IT, AND NELL ARDENT IS NOT IN THAT CHAPTER.**
- **and the borough is ordinary and the month is a month and not a turn of anything:** a hard frost that leaves black ice wherever a wall keeps the sun off, gone by about midday · the shop that shuts early up at twenty past seven, an hour later than the old time, and the radio shop beside it with its own shutter at half height, a man inside, a set on the counter and no volume · a man down with a shovel at the corner by the bank, and the grit bin at the top of Peverell Street with a crust in the bottom of it that was not there on Wednesday · a white van with a fridge standing in it that turned round at the bottom of Peverell Street in about thirty foot without reversing · a signless van at the end of Marsh Lane with an empty back and a tarpaulin over the top rail, on four days out of the six.
- **AND A CHAIR AND A BATH IN A YARD UNDER A GABLE END,** some six foot apart, the chair stood against the wall with a blanket over its back, and the ground under it gone hard and white, the bath on its side with rain in the side that faces out, and a man carrying tools who came through that yard at a quarter past two and left by the side passage and saw neither.
- **AND FOUR ANSWERS ARE STILL GOING ROUND THAT TABLE AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** Since the twelfth of January there have been four of them on that table and none has shifted since, and nobody in that room has ever managed to say what those four have between them, beyond their number and the fact of the table. The woman who works in a shop said that nobody has added to it and nobody has taken any off it, and that the street it leads off is the same street it led off before. The man in his forties agreed and looked at the boards of the table, and has a scrap in his pocket with a figure on it that he has shown to nobody. **THE MAN FROM THE PARADE STOOD UP AT ABOUT HALF PAST NINE, AND THE YOUNG WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY LOOKED AT HIM, AND THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP LOOKED AT HIM, AND THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES SET HIS CUP DOWN, AND HE STOOD THERE FOR ABOUT TWO SECONDS AND SAT DOWN AGAIN AND DRANK HIS TEA AND SAID THE TEA GOES COLD IN FOUR MINUTES EVERY WEEK, AND THEY BOTH SAID HE WOULD ASK IT THURSDAY.** That meter ran dry at about ten past ten and was locked off afterwards, and the porch light was burning when the door opened and died about twenty minutes later with the street lamp outside it.
- **NOBODY IS RESCUED. NOBODY DIES. NOBODY RENEWS ANYTHING. NOTHING WAS MINUTED. NOBODY WAS THANKED AND NOBODY REFUSED TO BE THANKED. THE ELEVEN STANDING REFUSALS ARE ELEVEN. CARD 0304 IS NOT A CLOSE AND IS NOT CALLED ONE.**

## 11. Still open, and still forbidden to Continuation 0012

Nothing in Volume 04 has been resolved, undone, improved on, restated or answered, and nothing in Batches 0001 to 0005 or in Continuations 0006, 0007, 0008, 0009, 0010 or 0011 may be. Nobody renews the order, nobody expires it, nobody renames the blank term, nobody puts a name in the line on page two, nobody fills the empty square, Roy Albery does not walk again, nobody dies, nobody is rescued by chance, nobody walks again, the eleven refusals are not twelve, the counter in the vestibule answers nothing and was not entered and was not in any scene, the rota is not rebuilt, the second line under Bram Osei's Thursday is not looked at, the register does not move, the letter, the card, the page, the printed sheets, the column, the shop-window card, the crate with a lid and the four bottles all stand, the drawer under the counter in the vestibule is not opened, is not counted, is not asked about and is not mentioned, and the trade-counter drawer is in no chapter of this batch either and holds two sheets and neither has been out since the nineteenth of July. **THE QUIET CUT, THE CIVIC ERASURE, THE ORIGIN OF THE COMMUNITY-WATCH TERM, THE THREE-LINE BRASS TOKENS, THE FIRST CHARTER, THE PROXY, THE ROOT CHAMBER, THE LOWGLASS FLOOR, THE BORROWED CITY AND THE NINE HINGES ARE NOT SPENT. FRANCES TOLLEY IS NOT NAMED AND THE READING RULE IS NOT USED. PRUE CALLOWAY'S FINDING IS NOT OPENED AGAIN AND THE CLAIM TO OPEN A DOOR IS SPENT. NO WEIR RESIDENT MAY BE DESCRIBED IN ANYTHING THAT LEAVES A STREET, AND THE FILLING ROUND DOWN COLLIER LANE IS NOT THE WEIR AND THE TWO MAY NEVER BE JOINED. COLIN WHARTON IS NOT NAMED AND NOBODY GOES UP THAT LANE. A WOMAN OF THIRTY-FOUR IS NOT NAMED, REFERENCED, GENDERED OR GIVEN A CONDITION. PARAGRAPH 4.11 IS NOT ANSWERED AND THE COMPLIANCE WEEK IS NOT VERIFIED. NO ROOT TERM EXPIRES AND THE RECLAMATION SEASON IS NOT SPENT, NOT ANTICIPATED AND NOT DESCRIBED AS APPROACHING.**

**AND NOTHING IN THIS BATCH MAY BE RE-SPENT BY THE NEXT ONE, AND THE FULL LIST OF IT IS AT §16 OF THE CONTINUATION 0011 BLOCK IN `state/continuity.md`. SPECIFICALLY: THE RAIL ON FOUR STEPS, THE ARM AND THE TIMBER, THE BATH IN THE YARD, THE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FOUR POUNDS AND THE TWO BOXES, THE SECOND TIME THAT QUESTION WENT ROUND A STREET, THE CHEST OF HINGES IN THE SHED, THE NINE THINGS ON A PAVEMENT AND THE BAG OF SALT, THE LAMP IN THE PORCH, THE SECOND QUESTION PUT TO THAT LAD, AND THE BINDER AGAINST AN OPEN BOOK. A HANDRAIL GOING BACK ON A WALL IS NOT A REPEAT TO BE RUN AGAIN. A CLOSER COMING OFF A DOOR IS NOT A REPEAT. A BATH IS NOT A REPEAT. A FOURTH SHEET IS NOT A REPEAT. A FIFTH WRONG ANSWER IS NOT A REPEAT AND MAY NOT BE INVENTED. A SIXTH ROOM FOR THAT LAD IS NOT A REPEAT AND THE CARD THAT ASKS FOR FIVE ROOMS MUST SAY WHAT IS NEW ABOUT THE FIFTH TIME.**

**AND THE FOUR FLAGS, ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE OF THEM A WRITER'S TO CLOSE. FIRST: THE NAME OF THE CITY. THE PROSE SAYS TIDEGLASS, ONCE IN EACH OF CHAPTERS 295–304 AND IN EVERY DATELINE, AND NO CHARACTER MAY NOTICE, MISNAME OR ASK ABOUT IT, AND NEITHER *NACRE* NOR *RIVER STACKS* APPEARS IN THOSE TEN CHAPTERS. SECOND: THE THIRD OF THE EIGHT PLACES IS PRINTED TWO WAYS IN THIS REPOSITORY AND WAS NOT TOUCHED IN CONTINUATION 0011 BECAUSE THE EIGHT PLACES ARE NOT READ OUT IN ANY OF THOSE TEN CHAPTERS, SO NEITHER SPELLING APPEARS AND THE FLAG CARRIES FORWARD UNDECIDED; **AND FIVE STATE FILES PRINT BOTH SPELLINGS — `state/continuity.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/batch-summaries.md` AND `state/live-canon.md` — ONE PRINTS ONLY ONE AND ONE PRINTS ONLY THE OTHER, WHICH IS THE MEASURED CENSUS OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 AND WHICH EVERY EARLIER BLOCK STILL GIVES AS FOUR AND STILL SAYS `state/live-canon.md` PRINTS NEITHER, AND WHERE THEY DISAGREE WITH THIS PARAGRAPH THIS PARAGRAPH IS RIGHT, BECAUSE A CORRECTION THAT ERASES ITS OWN ERROR LEAVES NO RECORD THAT A FIGURE WAS EVER WRONG. **A CHAPTER GOVERNS, A WRITER DID NOT DECIDE ANY OF IT, A HUMAN MAY RULE EITHER WAY AND MAY ALSO HAVE TO RULE ON 0243 AGAINST ITSELF, AND THIS PARAGRAPH IS THE PLACE THE FLAG IS CARRIED AND NO OTHER FILE MAY NAME A SPELLING OR ADD A CENSUS.**** THIRD: WHAT VOLUME 05 IS CENTRALLY. FOURTH: THE BOUNDARY — `outline/series.md` GIVES VOLUME 04 AS 145–192, VOLUME 05 AS 193–240, VOLUME 06 AS 241–288 AND VOLUME 07 AS 289–336, THE PAGE HAS 195 THROUGH 304 IN THE VOLUME 05 DIRECTORY, THE TWO DISAGREE AT BOTH ENDS, THERE IS NO FIGURE FOR THE END OF VOLUME 05 IN EITHER, THE FIGURE 242 IS USED BY NOBODY AND NOTHING MAY BE BUILT ON IT, NO CHAPTER IS MOVED BETWEEN DIRECTORIES, NO OUTLINE IS REWRITTEN, NO VOLUME 06 OUTLINE AND NO VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT EXISTS, AND CARD 0304 IS NOT A CLOSE.

**AND TWO CARD COLLISIONS WERE FLAGGED IN CONTINUATION 0011 AND THE RULE WAS KEPT BOTH TIMES. **CARD 0304 ASKED FOR *A BOROUGH, IN A SPRING*, AND TEN GAPS FROM TUESDAY 31 JANUARY 2017 CANNOT PUT ITS TENTH CHAPTER EARLIER THAN THURSDAY 2 MARCH 2017 OR LATER THAN TUESDAY 11 APRIL 2017, SO THE MIDDLE OF A SPRING CANNOT BE REACHED ON EITHER BOUND, AND 0304 TOOK THE DATE ITS OWN COUNT GAVE IT, WHICH IS FRIDAY 10 MARCH 2017, THE LATEST LEGAL DATE OF THE TEN, AND WAS GIVEN ORDINARY WEATHER INSTEAD, AND THE WORD *SPRING* IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS.** CARD 0298 PRINTED TWO FIGURES FOR ONE THING, A FOURTH COST IN ITS GOAL AND A THIRD COST IN ITS OWN MAJOR TURN, AND THE CHAPTER PRINTS A SHEET WITH A FIGURE ON IT AND DOES NOT NUMBER IT. **A REVIEWER WHO COUNTS GAPS MUST COUNT TEN AND NOT NINE, BECAUSE THE GAP BETWEEN 0294 AND 0295 IS THE FIRST OF THEM AND IS THE EASY ONE TO LEAVE OUT, AND THIS BATCH'S GAPS ARE 1, 3, 4, 4, 3, 5, 3, 3, 6 AND 7.****

**AND THE MEASURING PASSES ON CHAPTERS 295–304. **THE FIGURE CENSUS WAS BUILT FIRST AND RUN AGAIN AT THE END AND IT DID NOT RUN OUT: NO NEW FIGURE IN THIS BATCH WAS GIVEN AN AGE, THE FREE FIGURES REMAINING ARE WOMEN 18, 87 AND 90 AND NO MEN, AND THAT IS THE BLESSED ANSWER AND NOT A CLOSURE. THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES IS ONE MAN IN THREE CHAPTERS OF THIS BATCH, WHICH ARE 0295, 0300 AND 0301, AND WAS NOT SPLIT, AND THE MAN IN THE HI-VIS IS ONE MAN AND IS ON NOTHING. **THE WORD *THANK*, THE STEM *DRAIN-*, *HOSE*, *PANEL*, *LATTICE*, *PERMISSION*, *COMPACT*, *PACT*, *RESERVATION*, *ORDER*, *DRAWER*, *ENVELOPE*, *NACRE*, *RIVER STACKS*, *HALSEY CROSS*, *HALSE CROSS*, *WARDEN*, *RECLAMATION*, *SPRING*, *THE NINTH*, *FOUR YEARS*, *A LOAD-BEARING ANYTHING*, *A KEY MOMENT*, *A TURNING POINT* AND *A PIVOT* ARE ALL AT ZERO IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS. THE FIGURE *ELEVEN* IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN **WITH THE DATELINES REMOVED**, AND ITS ONE RAW OCCURRENCE IN THE TEN IS 0298'S DATELINE, *Saturday the eleventh of February 2017*, AND NOT A BODY. *A TUESDAY* IS 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0 AND 0 AGAINST A CAP OF TWO, AND THE FIRST CHAPTER OF THE BATCH IS DATED ON A TUESDAY, WHICH IS THE BINDING FLOOR'S FAULT AND NOT A WRITER'S. ONE DAY-COUNT IS PRINTED AND IT IS AT 0304 ON ITS OWN DATE AND IN THE BODY. TEN CHAPTERS CARRY TWO STANDALONE ALL-CAPS PARAGRAPHS EACH, TOTAL TWENTY, AGAINST A CAP OF FIVE, AND TWENTY `---` SCENE BREAKS, TWO IN EACH, AGAINST A CAP OF TWO IN EACH. *FOUR HUNDRED YARDS* IS 0 IN ALL TEN AGAINST A CAP OF TWO, **AND WAS PRINTED HERE AS 1 UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026, WHICH WAS WRONG: NO STRING *HUNDRED YARDS* EXISTS IN CHAPTERS 295–304.** TWENTY-THREE DEFECTS WERE CAUGHT IN THE CHAPTERS AND ALL TWENTY-THREE WERE REPAIRED AT THE SOURCE, AND THE FULL LIST IS §12 OF THE CONTINUATION 0011 BLOCK IN `state/continuity.md`. **THE VERBATIM-SPAN CHECK WAS RUN SIX TIMES AND EVERY WINDOW NAMED AND EVERY THRESHOLD NAMED, AND EVERY REUSE OF PROSE FOUND IN ANY WINDOW WAS REWRITTEN WHERE A CHAPTER COULD BE CHANGED AND IN THE STATE FILE WHERE THE FILE WAS WHAT DESCRIBED IT MORE CLEANLY THAN THE CHAPTER IS. **THE SIX RESULTS, GIVEN AS RAW SPANS AND AS REUSES OF PROSE, BECAUSE THE SECOND FIGURE IS THE ONE THAT BINDS: RUN ONE, ZERO AND NIL. RUN TWO, SIXTY-SIX ACROSS EIGHT TEXTS AND NIL. RUN THREE AGAINST 265–274, EIGHTEEN AND NIL; AGAINST 255–274, TWENTY-NINE AND NIL; AGAINST 285–294, ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTEEN AND NIL. RUN FOUR, FIFTY-EIGHT ACROSS SIX TEXTS AND NIL. RUN FIVE, SEVENTY-FOUR ACROSS THIRTEEN TEXTS AND NIL. EVERY SURVIVING SPAN IN EVERY WINDOW IS A DATELINE, A HOUSE FORM, A RECURRING BOROUGH NOUN OR A FIGURE'S OWN BY-NAME TAG, AND THE DATELINES AND BY-NAME TAGS ARE DETECTED AGAINST THE CHAPTERS' OWN DATELINE LINES AND THE CHAPTERS' OWN TAGS RATHER THAN AGAINST A LIST OF WORDS.** THE BIGGEST FINDING IN THE CHAPTERS WAS IN RUN THREE AGAINST CHAPTERS 285–294, THE TEN IMMEDIATELY ABOVE, WHERE TWO CHAPTERS OF THIS BATCH HAD BEEN WRITTEN IN THE CHAPTERS BEFORE THEM WORD FOR WORD, AND NO WINDOW NAMED IN THE PROMPT WOULD HAVE FOUND EITHER; THAT WINDOW NOW RETURNS ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTEEN RAW SPANS AND NIL. THE BIGGEST FINDING IN THE FILES WAS THAT THE FIRST DRAFT OF ALL FOUR HAND-OVER BLOCKS WAS ITSELF A FOURTH COPY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, WHICH IS WHAT RUN FOUR EXISTS TO CATCH, AND ALL FOUR BLOCKS WERE REWRITTEN RATHER THAN PATCHED, THREE OF THEM TWICE. **A FINAL PASS FOUND SIX MORE REUSES OF PROSE AFTER THE TWENTY-THREE OF §12 — FOUR PAIRS INSIDE THE CHAPTERS AND TWO LINES PRINTED IN STATE FILES — AND ALL SIX WERE REPAIRED, AND THE WORD COUNTS WERE TAKEN AGAIN AFTER THEM.** THE SECOND RUN AND THE FOURTH RUN ARE THE ONES THAT MATTER AND BOTH RETURN NIL REUSES OF PROSE. EVERY FIGURE IN THIS BLOCK IS TO BE RE-MEASURED FROM THE CHAPTERS AND NOT CARRIED FORWARD FROM HERE, BECAUSE EVERY REPAIR MOVES THE FIGURE, AND A FIGURE PRINTED BEFORE ITS OWN REPAIRS IS A FIGURE PRINTED BEFORE ITS OWN REPAIRS.** THE NEXT PHASE IS A CONTINUATION BATCH, CHAPTERS 305 ONWARD, DATED FROM SATURDAY 11 MARCH 2017, WHICH IS 0304'S OWN DATE PLUS ONE AND WHICH IS A SATURDAY, NOT A FRIDAY, AND WAS PRINTED HERE AS A FRIDAY UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026, AND IT IS NOT A VOLUME CLOSE UNTIL A HUMAN DECIDES THE BOUNDARY.


# VOLUME 05 OPEN THREADS — after Continuation 0012, Chapter 314, Monday 17 April 2017

**Read with the "VOLUME 05, CONTINUATION 0012 — CHAPTERS 305–314" block at the end of `state/continuity.md`, which carries the canon for these ten chapters and governs wherever the two differ, and with the "VOLUME 05, CONTINUATION 0011 — CHAPTERS 295–304" block above it, which still governs everything those ten chapters did. **VOLUME 05 IS NOT CLOSED AND IS NOT DECLARED CLOSED. THE SERIES IS AT 314 OF 720. NOTHING IN CONTINUATION 0012 RESOLVED ANYTHING AND NOTHING IN IT MAY BE UNDONE, IMPROVED ON, RESTATED OR ANSWERED. EVERY DAY-COUNT BELOW IS CALCULATED FROM ITS ANCHOR ON THE DAY IN QUESTION AND NOT CARRIED FORWARD FROM A SUMMARY: at Monday 17 April 2017 the blank line under the nineteenth is eight hundred and seventy-three days, the card in the slot is seven hundred and eighty-seven, and the letter in the tray is seven hundred and eighty-nine.**

## 1. An object left in an angle

- **MAJOR TURN: THE THING IS DONE, THE DAY CARRIES ON, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO FOUND ANYTHING OUT ABOUT IT WAS A MAN WITH A BOILER ON A BARROW WHO WAS NEVER IN THAT SHOP.** A man in his thirties took a pine door off three hinges at a house in a terrace off the Cleeve road, where a new leaf goes in on the twenty-eighth and a fitter had said the old one was his to take. It would neither lie flat in the van nor stand on end, the rear doors being shut and the seat not folding, so it went on its top edge into the angle where a back fence meets the end of a wall on a footpath behind the low end of Peverell Street, the letter plate to the fence. That path is the only way through, the kerb being raised outside those houses for the length of two cars. He told the woman who runs the shop behind that yard where it had gone; she said it was not their yard; he said that was why he was telling her.
- **The pushchair went round it,** one wheel at a time, with the child against her hip, on what was left of the way, and the fence marked the top of the paintwork on the way past. There was nobody there to speak to. She came back the same way at about ten past two and it had gone on a hired van with four pieces of offcut under it.
- **The light.** A man in his sixties, first floor, curtains open, a cup untouched and nothing to do, watched all of it from a window with a clear view of that fence. He went out of the room, came back, and switched on the light over that door. Nobody in that house is on the days, nobody went under that light that day or the next except her, and she had nothing to look up at.
- **The man who put the door there came back at about two for his own van, saw it from about six foot, got in and drove off, and did not cross over to look at the front of it.**
- **The man with the boiler.** He chose that lane instead of the parade, stopped in the middle of it, looked at the door for about ten seconds — on its top edge, with a scrape along the paintwork, nothing under it, nothing tying it, still standing after whatever weather had come in between — and then went back up and used the side entrance rather than the front. He was not going to ask a stranger about a door with a barrow and a boiler behind him. **The woman who runs that shop does not know her boiler came round the back, he did not tell her, nobody in that shop has ever found out about the door, and nobody went back to look at it, including the man who left it.** Nobody went and thanked anybody. A fact that happened is not a structure and not a precedent, and a man who is useful is on nothing.

## 2. One place further, and a figure that came back wrong

- **MAJOR TURN: THE SAME ANSWER GOES ONE PLACE FURTHER, A PERSON WHO HEARD IT IN THE FIRST PLACE HEARS THAT IT HAS GONE, A FIGURE LEAVES THAT HALL FOR A KITCHEN AND COMES BACK A DIFFERENT FIGURE, AND NEITHER FACT HELPS ANYBODY.** In the Methodist hall on Carrow Lane a man who repairs sewing machines said, unasked, that a woman in the second house of Marsh Lane has been sent a bill that would put her landlord in a position, and why: a meter swapped in November, the old unit on a different supply, and two supplies billing one kitchen. He goes to that hall most weeks, has for a good few years, and has never been in a room with a camera or anything that is going to be written down, which the chapter gives as the reason he says what he says.
- The woman who works in a shop told him not to say that in there. The man in his forties told him to leave it, and gave one reason: a spoken thing leaves a room on the people in it, and that woman cannot take hers back and did not put it there.
- **THE FIGURE, AND WHOSE IT WAS.** Half past eight, in the kitchen beside that hall, the young woman of about twenty at the sink produced a hundred and ninety pounds, and nobody had given her that figure. **It belonged to the woman who works in a shop, who had it from a queue at a post office the previous Friday — over two hundred, in two quarters running — and had said right at the time and told nobody for a fortnight.** Each of them worked out whose it was. The young woman said she does not need to be right and only needs to have heard it, and was answered that she is not going to be told she heard a thing she did not hear.
- The hall heard only that the conversation had gone into a kitchen, and no figure came out of it into the room. The man in his forties put a folded scrap into a pocket and covered the urn.
- At the end of that room a folding table was said to be standing on two feet of scaffold board that came out of a skip on Cleeve Street in the summer and holds it up. Nobody wrote it down and nobody took it off.
- **A hall on a meter is not a council, and a number said at a door is not the number on the paper. A FIFTH WRONG ANSWER ABOUT THE REGISTER WAS NOT PRODUCED, NONE OF THE FOUR WAS CORRECTED OR IMPROVED ON OR ADDED TO, THE FOURTH WAS NOT CALLED A FIFTH, AND THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JUNE WAS NOT PUT BACK TO FOUR.** The man who invented the first of those four answers is not in that room, is not told and must not be told.

## 3. A cupboard under a stair, and a disagreement

- **MAJOR TURN: THE THING GETS HALFWAY AND STOPS, TWO PEOPLE DISAGREE AND BOTH OF THEM HAVE A POINT, AND IT IS LEFT WHERE THE DISAGREEMENT HAPPENED.** A built-in cupboard under that first-floor stair, a flat door, a knob, no lock, and a key hanging inside it, on a hook, that has hung there since the building was finished. Inside: a wool fire blanket in a printed bag, a foot of flat steel, cable ties, wire, a bucket. The man in his thirties was there at about ten past seven about a box for a Monday job.
- **The blanket went into his bag. The steel went four inches in and stopped,** and he stood holding the bag and the steel out at either side, and would not put the steel up a sleeve and carry it like a bottle because of the light on that stair.
- The woman in the room next door along, counting past the fire door, whose own door stands open three inches with a chair against it, gave him her side of it: a cupboard with a bar and no blanket is short one thing, and every building in this town is short one thing or the other, and nobody has ever said a word about any of it; a cupboard with a blanket in it and nothing else in it is a thing somebody could make a noise about. He put the steel down. **She said she was not going to ask him to take it and then act as though she had not noticed, that she was not going to call it all right when it is not a thing she can call all right to, and that she was not going to put her name to a foot of steel for the rest of her life.** She also said she was not interested in discussing a foot of steel at half past seven on a Saturday.
- **Somebody else's arm was not used.** She offered to ask the woman at the end of that landing. He declined, and the chapter gives the reason, which is not the box: a screwdriver, a bag of plaster and nine words in her dressing gown in February are the reason.
- He went down that stair with the blanket; she put the chair back against her door. **The blanket lay on the arm of that chair for eight days.**
- On the Monday, at about half past eight, the man in his forties found that the bar on that fire door comes out about four foot and stops, nine inches short of the edge of the cupboard door, and that the cupboard it is meant to close has never had a lock on it and has never had anybody in that building come and look at it, the visiting round belonging to a firm from beyond the county boundary that comes twice a year and writes in a book. He put a hand on the bar, saw it halt in mid-air, said that two of them could make a noise about it, said right, and went downstairs. **He did not take the bar off, and the key is still on its hook, and the cupboard door is still a flat door with a knob on it.**
- A ladder is not a crew, a ladder is not a rescue, a trolley is not an arrangement, a man with a trolley is not a crew, and no trolley is in that chapter. Nobody may give anybody permission in that chapter and nobody did.

## 4. One bill, two figures, two strangers

- **MAJOR TURN: TWO PEOPLE DESCRIBE THE SAME HOUSE TO STRANGERS AND IT IS NOT THE SAME HOUSE BY THE SECOND ACCOUNT, AND NOBODY PUTS THEM TOGETHER.** On a parade, outside a shutter that stands at half height most days, the man who sells radios told the woman with a shopping bag that a bill in the second house of Marsh Lane is over two hundred, has been twice running, and is not a meter anybody has misread, and that the question to put to a landlord is which meter he read, on the ground that a landlord claiming to have read both has read a unit that is not there. What is on the paper is what a landlord may charge. The rest is not on the paper and will not be.
- **The other figure.** A man of about thirty had been at a shelter at the bottom of that parade for a while with a number on the display that had not moved, and had been given the same bill described at a hundred and ninety pounds, in February, by a man he worked with who has since gone to work thirty miles off. He kept it because it was said twice the same way, and he is a man who reads figures off the bottom of things and who was not going to invent one to be even with anybody. He was not talking about her bill and said so.
- They met at the shelter and did not settle it. He asked her straight out what the figure was. She said one of them is talking about a different house or a different quarter or one of them is wrong, and that she was not standing at a bus stop working it out, and that she has the paper and he was not being given it. He said he was not asking for the paper, and that he was not going to pretend he had not got the figure.
- **AND IT WENT ON BEING SAID.** The woman behind the counter of the shop that shuts early heard the whole of the first account and said none of it. Later that evening the man who sells radios put it again to a man buying a battery, who never met her and was gone inside four minutes. On the Saturday she went down that street past that shop and the two of them passed one another without a word. **he is unaware that anybody in this borough is carrying a figure for her kitchen at all, AND SHE DOES NOT KNOW THAT HE HAS BEEN GIVEN A FIGURE BY A MAN WHO HAS LEFT THE COUNTY, AND NEITHER HAS PUT THE OTHER'S FIGURE TO THE OTHER IN A WAY THAT COULD BE CHECKED.**
- A copy is not a wall and an envelope is not a tray, and a thing that is found is not a discovery, and nothing in that chapter was found.

## 5. A third thing, and something else on the same morning

- **MAJOR TURN: THE BUILDING DOES A THIRD THING TO THE SAME WOMAN AND SHE IS STILL IN THAT ROOM ON THE SAME TERMS, SOMEBODY ELSE PATCHES A STEP ON THAT MORNING, AND THE TWO ARE NOT CONNECTED BY ANYBODY.** The stop tap in that building stays shut from late Saturday morning until late Monday evening. The passage has a wooden tap case with a hinged little door, a board over the tap, and a strip fitting overhead. The riser is fixed to the wall of that outside stair, mid-way: a valve, a lock handle, an outlet with a coupling, a cap on a short chain, and four foot of pipe with a jet nozzle on the end.
- **THE THIRD THING, AND WHAT WAS LEFT OF IT.** No nozzle. The coupling had been capped with brass, a washer under it, wound on tight. **That is not the cap that came off the building: this building's cap is a moulded plastic one on a chain, and it lives in the cupboard under the stairs on the first floor, on a shelf beside a rolled wool blanket and a foot of steel, such things being kept together in a building of that kind and whoever filled that cupboard having been tidy.** The tap case was standing open and the strip fitting was lit, and nothing accounts for the passage light being on at that hour on a Sunday.
- **She stayed about a minute, closed the case, and carried the water up. THE PASSAGE HAD BEEN ENTERED BY SOMEBODY, AND NOBODY KNEW IT. NOBODY IN THAT BOROUGH KNOWS WHO CAPPED IT OR WHERE FOUR FOOT OF PIPE WENT, AND SHE DID NOT SAY IT TO A SOUL THAT DAY OR ON THE MONDAY AND HAS NOT SAID IT SINCE, AND SHE DID NOT PUT IT IN A LETTER.**
- **THE BLANKET, AT ABOUT TWO THAT AFTERNOON.** The woman in the room next door along brought out a rolled wool blanket that had been on the arm of that chair for about eight days, having asked nobody where it came from, having passed that landing daily in between, and the man who had left it had not returned to that stairway since the Saturday. She knocked on the door at the end of the corridor and gave her reasons: a good blanket, wool, no mark on it, a bit too big for the bed she has but warmer than what is on it, and the radiator in her own room has been off since before Christmas and is not coming on by itself. From where she stood she could see a tea towel and a cardigan. **She stayed out, no question was put to her, and she named no man, no stairway and no parcel.** The woman of about fifty-five took it in both arms, did not open it, and laid it across the foot of the bed, clear of the cardigan. She said it was kind of her and was told not to, that a spare has been sitting in that room since November, and that it could be left outside that door any time.
- **THE STEP, ON THE MONDAY MORNING AT ABOUT TEN TO EIGHT.** The man in his forties let himself in at that street door with mortar mix and a trowel. The front left corner of the step had been loose in its concrete since before Christmas, moving underfoot and making a noise like somebody knocking twice. He knocked it out, came it out about two inches, packed mix under it, drew the top up flush, put a boot on it and then his weight onto that boot, and the step neither moved nor sounded. **He did it before the first person down that passage had the tap open and told nobody in this borough that he had done it.**
- **A LADDER IS NOT A RESCUE, A GIFT IS NOT A PURCHASE, A PUMP IS NOT A RESCUE, AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FIVE IS NOT HOUSED BY ANYBODY IN THIS BATCH.** Nobody arranged the door of her room, nobody asked her about it, nobody told her who fitted the hasp on the inside of it, and nobody in that building has ever spoken to her about any part of that door. The brass cap is still on that outlet. The blanket is still on that bed. The step does not knock. The two are not connected.

## 6. Two yards, three doors and four weeks apart

- **MAJOR TURN: THE THING IS LEFT TWICE, THE SECOND TIME BY A MAN WHO HAS FORGOTTEN THE FIRST TIME, THE FIRST MAN IS NEVER ASKED, AND THE ONE PERSON WHO SAW BOTH NEVER PUT THEM TOGETHER.** A small iron bed frame, single, cream gone orange at the knuckles of the joints, a sprung base, a head and a foot, one slat missing out of about fourteen. Such things leave houses in this town several times a week.
- **First leaving, that Thursday morning,** in the yard behind the shop that shuts early, by the man in his thirties, off a back bedroom on a road off the Cleeve one, carried on the boards of the van since about the middle of the month. Against the gable wall on its side, head end up, foot end about three foot out into the yard. He said where it was going, and also said it would go in a skip later in the month and she would not see it, and he did not offer to come back for it.
- **The fortnight.** At that counter four days a week, and four times a day through the wired glass behind it, the woman who works in a shop had the frame in view. She did not go out into that yard. She did not ask where it came from and the woman who runs the shop did not ask her. That gate is keyed to nobody in the building, and nobody has been asked to hold one. In the fortnight he walked that parade perhaps nine times and did not go through the gate and did not turn it round. It went into the skip at about twenty to six that Monday, with pram things, a bicycle wheel and a television, and the man who puts the bins out said he had not known such a thing could go in a skip.
- **Second leaving, four weeks later,** out of a unit off the same parade, by a man of about twenty-eight who has had a frame of his own since about the middle of January and has been sitting on it, a folded blanket on the seat and two milk crates in front. His unit's roof drips onto the top rail of it, so he stood it on its side against brick in the yard of a shop three doors further up and cleared the wet leaves at the low end with a length of stick, a barrow not fitting underneath. Nobody saw him. The landlord's men took that yard on the Monday, with a skip, and nobody asked them to.
- **He looked at the wall on the Tuesday and said nothing to anybody, then or since, AND HE DID NOT KNOW THAT THE OTHER FRAME HAD COME OUT OF A HOUSE OFF THE CLEEVE ROAD THREE WEEKS EARLIER AND BEEN STOOD AGAINST A WALL THREE DOORS BACK THE OTHER WAY. From the parade, through a gate on its latch, the second frame was seen by the woman who works in a shop, STOOD ON THE PAVEMENT LONG ENOUGH TO TAKE IN WHAT IT WAS, THE FRAME BEHIND HER OWN SHOP NOT ENTERING HER HEAD, AND WENT ON TO THE BUS STOP. BOTH WENT INTO SKIPS FOUR DAYS APART and the gate is still on a latch only.**

## 7. A thing promised out loud, twice, and not done

- **MAJOR TURN: HE SAYS HE WILL DO IT, HE DOES NOT DO IT, SOMEBODY ELSE SAYS HE SAID HE WOULD, HE DENIES SAYING IT, AND BOTH OF THEM ARE BEING STRAIGHT.** The step belongs to a shop at that street's low end, shut since the summer before last, and there is no freeholder of it in this borough to send an invoice to. Its outside corner has sunk until there is a lip about the height of two fingers, and it is where a right foot comes off the pavement, and the top of it is sheeted under a length of blue lino come loose at the near edge. He had said it in February as the fourth of nine things on that pavement.
- **HE SAID IT TWICE, IN THE SAME WORDS, THE SECOND TIME LOUDER, AND ON THE SECOND TIME A WOMAN WAS TEN FOOT AWAY WITH HER BACK TO THE ROAD.** On the Tuesday morning, on his knees with a new plug in each of the old holes, he found it to be a lip and not a hole, and said he would take it up, screed it, and have it done inside a fortnight; a man in a fleece going to the works, about eight foot off, said aye, and also said that a screed in that step would be indistinguishable from its neighbours in ten years and that the man responsible would not be him. **On the Wednesday morning at about ten past eight he said the same thing to a different man at that open end, the woman keeping her count nearby with her back turned, and had the pad of work notes out of his coat for the first time since the summer, took the elastic off with his teeth, wrote one line in pencil on the top sheet, and put the elastic back on.**
- **HE DID NOT DO IT THAT WEEK OR THE NEXT, and by the middle of April that lino had settled onto the tarmac of its own accord and lay flat enough for nobody to catch a toe on it, AND THE STEP IS STILL TWO INCHES DOWN AND WILL GO ON BEING TWO INCHES DOWN.** His reason is not a good one and the chapter says so: he can screed a step and charge for it, there is nobody here to invoice, and a man who raises a step for a shop he has no trade with becomes, to that shop, a stranger who has been near it, and the next thing that follows is a woman coming out of the parade and asking him about it. He settled on a Sunday when the parade would stand empty, and did not turn up on it either, and did not think about it again until the April bank holiday, when it rained.
- **THE HALL, ON THE THURSDAY.** The woman with a clipboard was on a folding stool at the open end of that hard with the board on her knee, her easel having gone round the back of the bank in February, and her course finishing with the end of April. She said that on the Tuesday, in the hour and a half before the rain, she counted four hundred and something, and that two men stood at the top of that lane, one of them going to the works, and the talk was of the same step, and that the man in his thirties said he would take it up in a voice you could hear over a bus. The man in his thirties came in at about ten to nine and denied having said he would do it. **He said he had said he would have a look at it, and that a look costs a boot and about two minutes while the work costs a whole Sunday that does not come back. She said she does not need to be right and only needs to have heard it, and that she is not going to be told she heard a thing she did not hear, being on that hard daily since the middle of January, and able to say what occurred on any given day of it. The man in a fleece said that is what he heard and that is what he told the woman with the board he had heard it, and that he could not say what the man in his thirties was doing at that lane because he goes to the works and his hours are not the same.** He laid the pad on that table face up for about a second, one pencil line on the top sheet and nothing else on it, and pocketed it again, and said nobody had asked him to do a step, that nobody was waiting on it, and that there is nothing in it and a step is a step.
- **A LIST IS NOT A DOCUMENT, A LIST IS NOT A FORM, THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS BOROUGH, A PAD WITH AN ELASTIC ROUND IT IN THE FRONT POCKET OF A VAN IS NOT A FORM, AND A THING SAID OUT LOUD IS NOT A THING ANYBODY KEEPS.** Nobody was given a title, a grade, a department, a chair, a seat, a membership, a form, a row on a rota or a way of being said anything about. Nobody in that room asked for a copy and nobody has a copy. On the Monday after, she handed the day's figures in at the back of a bank and kept nothing of her own. The step is still down, and the man in his thirties is on nothing for having said he would.

## 8. A pan taken in, used once, put away

- **MAJOR TURN: THE THING IS TAKEN IN AND USED ONCE AND PUT SOMEWHERE ELSE, AND THE PERSON WHO TOOK IT IN IS NOT THE PERSON IT WAS FOR.** A saucepan about eight inches across and four deep, riveted handle, black never properly out of one spot, standing on its side beside an open skip on a skip lorry with about nine inches of frozen water in the bottom. No lid near it and none in the house it came out of either. Stamped into the base: a letter, six figures, and a date reading twenty sixteen. The six figures are not anybody's name, not a postcode and not a number anybody here has a use for, and there is no maker's name on the object at all.
- **The carrier held one item of knowledge about the night worker, namely that he once stood in a coal queue at that lane's end in January and carried nothing off.** A coal line is not where a man is at half past five on a Saturday. The hall off that parade is. He was let in and stood by the counter about four minutes with it in his hand, offered no drink, and asked for none, and he asked who the man was and was told none of them and that he comes in about half past ten. He said it was for him, that the code is no use to anybody, and that the year on the base is the only thing about it worth knowing. He put it on the end of the table by the door with the handle inwards and went out into the rain. **Nobody in that room mentioned a saucepan.**
- **IT WAS NOT PUT AWAY WHEN IT WAS BROUGHT IN.** The woman who works in a shop had not put it in the cupboard with the urn and had not taken it to the kitchen and left it where it was. The night man arrived at about ten past ten, still in his coat, said there was no name on it, was told he never gave the man his name, turned it over, read the stamp without saying it out loud, and said aye, well.
- **AND IT WAS USED ONCE AND PUT SOMEWHERE ELSE.** At about ten to midnight, on the cooker at the rear of that parade's kitchen, about a third of a tin out of the tin, eaten standing up, washed out, dried on the cloth, and put at the back of the shelf above the lids, behind two lids that do not go with anything, because what is not his does not lie about a table in a full room. **He told nobody that he had used it. On the Wednesday she went in, the pan was not on the table, and she did not ask where it had gone. On the Thursday the man with the van was at the end of Marsh Lane with a tarpaulin over an empty back, and no word reached him about a pan in a cupboard in a kitchen.**
- A gift that is not refused is not a purchase, a person who is not counted is not a person who is helped, being let in is not a rescue, and being turned away is not a falling-out. The two of them were entirely civil and the pan is a pan.

## 9. Six days, five rooms, and not one question

- **MAJOR TURN: NOBODY ASKS HIM ANYTHING AT ALL, AND HE SAYS SOMETHING ANYWAY, AND NOBODY ANSWERS IT, AND HE GOES HOME.** Six days rather than one, in the same five rooms as last time — a kitchen at the back of a parade, the floor of the shop that shuts early on that parade, a kitchen in Corrance Lane, a corridor on the first floor of a building by the water, and a hall off that parade — his employer's kitchen having no employer from the Monday, and the woman not wanting him back in there at the end of it. The hot water on that side of the parade runs off a timer, and on a Saturday the timer is set wrong.
- **NOT ONE QUESTION, ALL DAY.** In the shop on the Tuesday he was told chairs up, carried seven through and set them where they go, stopped with the eighth in each hand and waiting to hear where it went, was given three words, had the stack looked at, was answered two hundred years, and was told there might be more on the Saturday if the weather held. On the Thursday he was told the delivery was off and to come in earlier. **A man at the end of that counter had a paper and a pen all morning and said nothing at all from one end of the day to the other.** In the hall, on his third pass with the tray, he stopped deliberately in the gap by the table, and the man in his forties — who asked him a question about two places in an earlier batch — looked up and then looked back down at his cup, and nothing came. He went round four times. The only conversation of the evening was about the meter in that porch wanting seeing to before the summer, and the other agreeing.
- **THE THING HE DID AND WAS NOT ASKED FOR.** Three bottles of washing-up, full, standing on that first-floor landing in the middle of the week, the building having been dry for a day and a half. His bucket was going to hit them. He carried them down one at a time to the bin store at the foot of that stair, put them on the shelf, and came back and mopped. She came out while he was finishing and said she would be in.
- **ON THE CORRIDOR, AT ABOUT TEN PAST THREE ON THE THURSDAY, HE TOLD THE WOMAN IN THE ROOM NEXT DOOR ALONG THAT THERE HAD BEEN A FIRE BLANKET IN THE CUPBOARD UNDER THE STAIRS IN A BAG, THAT THERE IS A BAR OF IRON UNDER IT, THAT THERE IS a key hanging on a hook inside that door, THAT A MAN HAD BEEN AND TAKEN THE BLANKET OUT, THAT HE SAW IT ON THE CHAIR OUTSIDE HER DOOR ON THE SATURDAY AND ON THE MONDAY IT WAS NOT ON THE CHAIR, AND THAT YOU DO NOT HAVE TO LOCK IT AND NOBODY DOES. SHE SAID NOTHING AT ALL, and then turned her eyes to the far wall, AND HE WENT DOWN THE STAIR AND OUT. **THAT BLANKET IS ON THE END OF A BED AT THE END OF THAT CORRIDOR, AND TO SAY ANYTHING AT ALL SHE WOULD HAVE TO NAME WHERE IT WENT, AND SHE WILL NOT, AND SHE HAS TOLD NOBODY IN THAT BUILDING.
- **AND ON THE BUS.** Half full. He got on at the bottom of the parade and went up to the top deck, where a man by the front with a folded paper. The lad said that the step outside the shop that is shut is still down, that it is nobody's to be doing, and that he has not done it and is not saying he is going to. The man identified it as the one with the lino on it, said aye, and went back to his paper. He got off at the top of the parade and walked.
- The pan in that kitchen was on its shelf the whole time he was there and was not touched, and neither he nor the man who works nights has said one word about it since. **He is on the dishes, he has no title, grade, department, chair, seat, membership, form or row on a rota, being asked is not a standing, being useful is not a job and being in a room is not being a member, and the question he asked on the fourth of June 2016 was not put to anybody in that room again and was not answered by anybody in it.** A lad in five rooms is not a crew, a sixth day is a device and not a finding, and no chapter of this batch contains a thanks of any kind.

## 10. An ordinary Monday in April

- **THE REGISTER IS AT NINETEEN, THE LINE UNDER IT IS EIGHT HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-THREE DAYS AND IS STILL BLANK, AND THAT IS THE ONE DAY-COUNT IN THE PROSE OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** On the Monday, at about half past eight, it went back onto a shelf in a room by the water in that building, having been leaned against by a binder on the tenth of March on a stair, by a woman who was unaware of what lay under it. On the Wednesday she carried the binder about two feet along to the next bay, the register following it an inch before stopping, and she squared the pair up. **The bay door does not catch and never has. THE LAST ENTRY IS A YARD LAMP, ITS BULB IN A COLUMN KEPT FOR MAINTENANCE, REPORTED OUT ABOUT NOVEMBER TWO YEARS AGO AND NOT NEAR SINCE, AND BELOW THAT the blank rule below that last entry runs about two fingers and then stops, leaving some two inches of the page itself empty above the foot of a book of that depth. NOBODY IN THAT ROOM LOOKED AT ANY OF IT. TWO PEOPLE WERE IN THAT ROOM AND BOTH WERE IN A HURRY. ON THAT CORRIDOR STANDS A DOOR, AND BEHIND IT A SHELF, AND ON THE SHELF A BAG TIED ROUND WITH STRING, TWICE OVER, AND THE BAG HAS NOT SHIFTED SINCE BEFORE THE WINTER.**
- **and the borough is ordinary and the month is a month and not a turn of anything:** rain off the estuary that hangs in the air and soaks a coat, the boards on the parade through to wet by about nine and wet all day · the shop that shuts early up at about ten to eight, roughly its time since the middle of March · a radio shop beside it with a shutter of its own at about chest-high, a man inside it and the set silent · the man who comes down with the shovel not coming down that parade until about two, and doing nothing at the bank end · a gritting bin at the top of Peverell Street with a skin over what is left in the bottom and a hole punched through one side about the size of a closed hand · a white van that turned into that street and stopped by the second house, where a man got out and looked up the front of a building in the rain for about a minute before it went back the way it came · the van with no sign on it at the end of Marsh Lane on most days of any week, tarpaulin bundled over the top rail and nothing behind it · a step outside a shop shut since the summer still down about two inches at the outside corner, its lino lying flat where the rain put it back and the crack at the other end gone over with grit.
- **AND FOUR ANSWERS ARE STILL GOING ROUND THAT TABLE AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PUT BESIDE ANY OTHER.** Since the twelfth of January there have been four of them on that table, and those still there are the ones that were there in January, and no one has ever managed to set any one of the four beside any of the other three, and and nobody at that table has been able to say what the four amount to together. **THE WOMAN WHO WORKS IN A SHOP SAID NOBODY HAS PUT A FIFTH ONE ON AND NOBODY HAS TAKEN ONE OF THE FOUR OFF AND NOBODY HAS SAID WHERE ANY OF THEM CAME FROM, AND that the one concerning a street concerns the same street it did in January, AND THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES SAID THAT WAS ALL OF IT.** She also said that a man had put the first of the four out loud at a shelter on that lane in February's wind, that the woman keeping the count answered that she had heard it from him first, and that nothing of a fifth kind has been produced by anybody since, including nobody at that table. The man in his forties said which would be the thing, and she said that would be the thing. The grate is full of old ash and has not been lit, and the folding table has two feet of scaffold board across its legs where a joint has opened, out of a skip on Cleeve Street in the summer.
- **THE MAN FROM THE PARADE PUT HIS QUESTION AGAIN, IN NEW WORDS, AND 0299 IS THE CHAPTER THAT HAS HIM SAY IT.** He said, to the table and to nobody, that somebody will one day lay a hand on the one that counts; that a council is not where it will come from, there being none in this town for years; that it will not come from the men who did the things, those men being gone; and that it will be a piece of paper somebody puts away in a folder and forgets. He said the subject itself does not interest him, only the being present. **WHERE 0314 AND 0299 DIFFER IN THE WORDING OF THAT SENTENCE, THE CHAPTER GOVERNS AND A LATER WRITER MUST NOT TREAT 0314 AS REPLACING 0299.** The man in his sixties said something was happening at the bank with the wet on that lamp, and the table let it lie.
- The gas ran out at about ten past ten and the case was padlocked afterwards, the chairs went up, she was among the last out, and the porch lamp was lit as the door opened and went out some fifteen minutes afterwards. That gas has come out of one woman's own money since the summer and from nowhere else.
- **NOBODY IS RESCUED. NOBODY DIES. NOBODY RENEWS ANYTHING. NOTHING WAS MINUTED. NOBODY WAS THANKED AND NOBODY REFUSED TO BE THANKED. THE ELEVEN STANDING REFUSALS ARE ELEVEN. CARD 0314 IS NOT A CLOSE AND IS NOT CALLED ONE.**

Nothing in Volume 04 has been resolved, undone, improved on, restated or answered, and nothing in Batches 0001 to 0005 or in Continuations 0006, 0007, 0008, 0009, 0010, 0011 or 0012 may be. FOURTH: THE BOUNDARY — `outline/series.md` GIVES VOLUME 04 AS 145–192, VOLUME 05 AS 193–240, VOLUME 06 AS 241–288 AND VOLUME 07 AS 289–336, THE PAGE HAS 195 THROUGH 314 IN THE VOLUME 05 DIRECTORY, THE TWO DISAGREE AT BOTH ENDS, THERE IS NO FIGURE FOR THE END OF VOLUME 05 IN EITHER, THE FIGURE 242 IS USED BY NOBODY AND NOTHING MAY BE BUILT ON IT, NO CHAPTER IS MOVED BETWEEN DIRECTORIES, NO OUTLINE IS REWRITTEN, NO VOLUME 06 OUTLINE AND NO VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT EXISTS, AND CARD 0314 IS NOT A CLOSE.

**AND TWO FLAGS WERE TAKEN IN CONTINUATION 0012 AND BOTH RULES WERE KEPT. **CARD 0314 ASKED FOR *A BOROUGH, IN A MONTH*, WHICH ITS OWN COUNT COULD GIVE IT, AND THE RULE THAT BINDS WAS APPLIED ANYWAY: THE WORD *SPRING* IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS, NO CHAPTER CALLS ITS OWN MONTH A TURN, A CHANGE OF FORTNIGHT OR A CHANGE OF ANYTHING, AND 0314 WAS GIVEN ORDINARY WEATHER INSTEAD, WHICH IS RAIN. **AND THE PROMPT'S OWN ARITHMETIC AT THE SHORT END IS INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT: IT COUNTS TEN GAPS, WHICH IS CORRECT, AND THEN MEASURES THE SHORT BOUND AS THE FLOOR PLUS THIRTY DAYS, WHICH TREATS THE INCOMING ONE-DAY FLOOR GAP AS A THREE-DAY GAP; ON STRICT ARITHMETIC TEN GAPS OF WHICH ONE IS FORCED TO ONE DAY GIVE A MINIMUM SPAN OF TWENTY-EIGHT DAYS AND A SHORTEST LEGAL TENTH CHAPTER OF SATURDAY 8 APRIL 2017. A FLOOR THAT IS TOO HIGH IS A PERMISSIVE ERROR AND NOT A MERE TYPO BECAUSE IT LETS A WRITER COMPRESS BELOW THE THREE-DAY MINIMUM, SO THE LATER AND SAFER PAIR WAS KEPT AND 0314 WAS PLACED AT MONDAY 17 APRIL 2017, WHICH SATISFIES BOTH PAIRS. THE MAN IN HIS THIRTIES IS ONE MAN IN FOUR CHAPTERS OF THIS BATCH, WHICH ARE 0305, 0307, 0310 AND 0311, AND THE MAN IN HIS FORTIES IS ONE MAN IN SEVEN, AND NEITHER WAS SPLIT, AND THE MAN IN THE HI-VIS IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH. **THE WORD *THANK*, THE STEM *DRAIN-* AND THE STEM *REQUIR-*, *HOSE*, *PANEL*, *LATTICE*, *PERMISSION*, *COMPACT*, *PACT*, *RESERVATION*, *ORDER*, *DRAWER*, *ENVELOPE*, *NACRE*, *RIVER STACKS*, BOTH SPELLINGS OF THE THIRD OF THE EIGHT PLACES, *WARDEN*, *RECLAMATION*, *SPRING*, *THE NINTH*, *FOUR YEARS*, *A LOAD-BEARING ANYTHING*, *A KEY MOMENT*, *A TURNING POINT* AND *A PIVOT* ARE ALL AT ZERO IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS.
